# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1977

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā

There is a secret to understanding Vedic knowledge:

*yasya deve parā bhaktir*  
*yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ*  
*prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**“Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the tenets of the Vedic scriptures automatically revealed.” \[*****ŚU*** **6.23\]**

If we at all want to follow the Vedic path to knowledge, we must observe the spiritual master’s appearance day with proper veneration. Vyāsa-pūjā is the special *pūjā* (worship) named after Vyāsa, the supreme authority on the Vedic literature, and offered to the spiritual master on his birthday.

As the empowered literary incarnation of Godhead, Vyāsa compiled all the *Vedas, Puraṇās, Upaniāds, and Mahābhārata* on the instructions of Narada, his spiritual master. Narada learned the Vedic wisdom from Brahma, who originally received it from the Supreme Lord Viṣṇu in his heart. The same transcendental knowledge comes in *paramparā,* the unbroken chain of disiplic succession, from Vyāsa through Madhva and others to Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu.

Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu, the most munificient incarnation of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, transmitted the essence of Vedic knowledge by means of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *maha-mantra*. As Lord Caitanya Himself explained to Prakaśananda Sarasvati:

*guru more mūrkha dekhi' karila śāsana*  
*mūrkha tumi, tomāra nāhika vedāntādhikāra*  
*'kṛṣṇa-mantra' japa sadā,—ei mantra-sāra*

**“My spiritual master considered Me as a fool, and therefore he chastised Me. ‘You are a fool,’ he said. ‘You are not qualified to study Vedanta philosophy, and therefore You must always chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. This is the essence of all** ***mantras*** **or Vedic hymns.’”** **\[*****Cc. Ādi*** **7.71\]**

During the past century, Lord Caitanya’s pure representatives Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Thakura and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura planted the seeds of a movement to spread the *maha-mantra* all over the world. Those seeds have fructified in the form of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada, who has, in the past decade, initiated thousands of disciples for more than thirty countries in the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda is the most worthy recipient of Vyāsa-pujā because only he has given Vyāsadeva’s literatures to so many without changing the meaning one iota. In fact, four hundred fifty years ago, the great ācārya Śrīla Vrndāvana dāsa Thākura wrote that in the future, representatives of Vyāsadeva would elaborately describe the pastimes of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, a statement later confirmed by Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākur. By reading the publication of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmrta*, we can understand that he is such a representative. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books prompted one Indian scholar to write, “Upon seeing the work of Swami Bhaktivedanta, one is tempted to wonder whether he is not the same Vyāsa reborn to present his literatures in a universal language for modern man.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as your disciples, we are offering our very inadequate glorifications of you in the pages of this Ninth Annual ISKCON Vyāsa-pujā book. We simply pray that you will find some pleasure in our meager efforts, for that is the actual meaning of Vyāsa-pujā.

His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, the foremost devotee and scholar of the previous age, is the spiritual master of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. He is the ocean of transcendental mercy and the bestower of devotional service upon the fallen souls. His is the deliverer of devotional service which is enriched with the conjugal love of Śrī-Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. He is the personified teachings of Lord Caitanya. Thus, he never tolerates any statement against the teaching of devotional service as enunciated by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī.

In 1936, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda delivered this poem as a Vyāsa-Pūjā homage for His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī

Adore adore ye all  
The happy day,  
Blessed than heaven,  
Sweeter than May,  
When he appeared at Puri  
The holy place,  
My Lord and Master  
His Divine Grace.

Oh! my Master  
The evangelic angel,  
Give us Thy light,  
Light up Thy candle.  
Struggle for existence  
A human race.  
The only hope  
His Divine Grace.

Misled we are  
All going astray.  
Save us Lord  
Our fervent pray.  
Wonder Thy ways  
To turn our face.  
Adore Thy feet  
Your Divine Grace.

Forgotten Krishna  
We fallen souls,  
Paying most heavy  
The illusion’s toll.  
Darkness around  
All untrace.  
The only hope  
His Divine Grace.

Message of service  
Thou hast brought.  
A healthful life  
As Chaitanya wrought.  
Unknown to all  
It’s full of brace.  
That’s your gift  
Your Divine Grace.

Absolute is sentient  
Thou hast proved,  
Impersonal calamity  
Thou hast moved.  
This gives us a life  
Anew and fresh.  
Worship Thy feet  
Your Divine Grace.

Had you not come  
Who had told  
The message of Krishna  
Forceful and bold.  
That’s your right.  
You have the mace.  
Save me a fallen  
Your Divine Grace.

The line of service  
As drawn by you  
Is pleasing and healthy  
Like morning dew.  
The oldest of all  
But in new dress.  
Miracle done  
Your Divine Grace.

## The World Teacher of Kṛṣṇa Consciousness

*kali-kālera dharma-kṛṣṇa-nāma-saṅkīrtana*  
*kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana*  
*Caitanya-caritāmrta, Antya 7.11-12)*

His Divine Grace  
A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda  
Founder-Ācārya of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness

FULFILLING THE ORDERS of his own spiritual master and of Lord Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda is enlightening the world in the science of *bhāgavata-dharma*-unalloyed devotional service to Lord Śri Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Thousands of years ago, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa descended in Bharata-varśa-India to impart the essence of Vedic knowledge: that the highest duty of every living being is to abandon all other engagements and surrender unto Him. Later, nearly five hundred years ago, Kṛṣṇa appeared in India again, as Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, to demonstrate, by His own example, how to surrender to Kṛṣṇa and how to love Kṛṣṇa. This mission of Lord Caitanya’s was firmyl established-on an unshakable foundation of Vedic evidence-by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmi Prabhupāda. Within recent history, this mission was preserved and enriched by Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Thākura, and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Gosvāmi Mahṛāja Prabhupāda. Now, by the infallible desire of Lord Kṛṣṇa and His representative *ācāryas* in the disciplic succession, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, the Founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, has carried this eternal mission beyond the borders of India and is establishing it around the world.

Those who have lost their vision because of the dense darkness of this age of Kali can receive light from His Divine Grace.

As Ācārya, Śrīla Prabhupāda has shown his disciples—and the world—what it is to dedicate oneself fully to preaching the message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Heedless of personal comfort, His Divine Grace endeavors unceasingly to bestow Kṛṣṇa’s mercy upon the fallen souls of this age. In his compassion, he is immersed in the mood of Prahlāda Mahārāja, who prayed to Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva:

*naivodvije para duratyaya-vaitaraṇyās*  
*tvad-vīrya-gāyana-mahāmṛta-magna-cittaḥ*  
*śoce tato vimukha-cetasa indriyārtha-*  
*māyā-sukhāya bharam udvahato vimūḍhān*  
*(SB 7.9.43)*

**“O best of the great personalities, I am not at all afraid of material existence, for wherever I stay I am fully absorbed in thoughts of Your glories and activities. My concern is only for the fools and rascals who are making elaborate plans for material happiness and maintaining their families, societies and countries. I am simply concerned with love for them.”**

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu declared:

*prṭhivīte āche yata nagarādi grāma*  
*sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma*  
*\[CB Antya-khaṇḍa 4.126\]*

**“IN AS MANY villages and towns as there are on the surface of the world, My name will be known.”**

To fulfil Lord Caitanya’s prediction, Śrīla Prabhupāda has established more than one hundred centers of the *saṇkīrtana* movement—each with affiliated traveling parties—to spread Kṛṣṇa’s glories throughout the world.

FOR THOUSANDS OF YEARS in India, people have celebrated Kṛṣṇa as the Supreme Godhead, the Lord of the universe. Now, by the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda, hundreds and thousands of people celebrate the glories of Lord Jagannātha in cities all over the world. Śrīla Prabhupāda is teaching the whole world how all living beings can live together as one in harmony and joy—by serving the Lord of the universe.

Śrīla Prabhupāda is presenting a cultural movement for the respiritualization of all human society. Music, dance, theatre, cinema—all have value when glorifying Kṛṣṇa only. Presented under Śrīla Prabhupāda’s expert transcendental guidance, India’s imperishable heritage of genuine spiritual culture is being appreciated throughout the world, not just as exotic entertainment but as a profoundly meaningful way to understand and approach the Supreme Personality of Godhead. In a few months, Bombay will witness the opening of ISKCON’s Center for Vedic Knowledge and Culture, which will include a Vedic temple, a 450-seat theatre, a recording studio, a library, a prasādam restaurant, dioramas, and a comfortable 8-story International Guest House. This will be yet another of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s great contributions to the cultural life of India, and of the world.

IN INDIA, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Thākura staged transcendental theistic exhibitions, using dioramas to illustrate the ideas and events recorded in the Vedic literature. Following in the footsteps of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Thākura, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda has commissioned his disciples to stage similar presentations in the West. Śrīla Prahupāda’s First American Theistic Exhibition (F.A.T.E) uses not only traditional figures formed of straw and clay, but also sophisticated computer-controlled audiovisual effects.

TO DEMONSTRATE he practical value of “simple living and high thinking,” Śrīla Prabhupāda has established twelve Kṛṣṇa conscious farm communities in North America, South America, Europe, Australia and India. By protecting cows, growing crops and, in all activities, worshiping Kṛṣṇa, Śrīla Prabhupāda’s disciples in these communities are illustrating the simple Vedic way to solve all economic problems and ultimately return home, back to Godhead, thus attaining the perfection of human life.

*kaumāra ācaret prājño*  
*dharmān bhāgavatān iha*  
*durlabhaṁ mānuṣaṁ janma*  
*tad apy adhruvam arthadam*  
*(Srimad-Bhagavatam 7.6.1)*

RECOGNIZING that nowhere in the world are there schools where children may be trained in the high Vedic standards of purity, sense control, and Kṛṣṇa consciousness, Śrīla Prabhupāda has reintroduced the old Vedic system of education—the Gurukula system—by which children are not only materially educated but also spiritually trained so that they may attain the highest perfection of life. Aside from small ISKCON Gurukula schools throughout the world, Śrīla Prabhupāda has now established the large, fully equipped Bhaktivedanta Gurukula in the holy city of Vrīndāvana for the children of ISKCON devotees and life members.

ŚRĪLA PRABHUPĀDA is presenting the message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness so convincingly and scientifically that even scientists have stepped forward to becomes his disciples. Some of these scientists have formed the Bhaktivedanta Institute, an educational society of Ph.D.’s dedicated to presenting the Vedic scientific understanding to the general scientific community through disciplines such as physics, chemistry, biology and mathematics. Speakers from the Institute travel widely, and this fall the Institute is sponsoring in Vrīndāvana a scientific convention on the subject “Life Comes from Life.” Among the scientific monographs published by the Institute is *Demonstration by Information Theory that Life Cannot Arise from Matter* by Sadāpūta dāsa Adhikārī (Richard Thompson, Ph.D.), one of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s disciples.

*Athāto Brahmā Jignāsa*

BHAKTIVEDANTA INSTITUTE

HIS DIVINE GRACE Śrīla Prabhupāda has established one hundred three temples of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa in thirty-six countries in North America, South America, Europe, Australia, Africa and Asia.

Each temple holds regularly scheduled worship of the Deity, sponsors *kīrtan,* distrubution of *prasāda,* and classes in *Bhagavad-gitā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* for the general public, and provides living facilities for devotees and guests. In short, the temples are like oases in the desert of māyā. And, most important, each temple is a preaching centre from which Śrīla Prabhupāda’s disciples go out to chant the glories of Kṛṣṇa and distribute Kṛṣṇa consciousness literature, following in the footsteps of their spiritual master.

IN EACH of ISKCON’s temples, Śrīla Prabhupāda has established the Deity of the Lord, who is duly worshiped according to *pāñcarātrikī*regulations by initiated *brāhmanas* among Śrīla Prabhupāda’s disciples. From 4 A.M. to 10 P.M., Śrīla Prabhupāda’s disciples are engaged in rendering different kinds of service to the Deity. Śrīla Prabhupāda has given a summary study of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmi’s *Bhakti-rasāmrta-sindhu* entitled *The Nectar of Devotion* to guide his disciples in the basic principles for worshiping the Deity.

IN ADDITION to etablishing temples in cities throuhgout the world, Śrīla Prabhupāda has also inspired his disciples to travel from city to city, town to town, taking the Deity of the Lord with them, to preach the message of Kṛṣṇa everywhere.

AMONG ŚRĪLA PRABHUPĀDA’S most important contributions are the International Centers he has established in holy cities of India so that visitors from around the world may go there to learn about the spiritual culture of India’s Vedic heritage. In Vṛīndavana, where Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa performed his youthful pastimes five thousand years ago, His Divine Grace has established the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma temple and International Guesthouse, already a place of pilgrimage for visitors both from India and from abroad. Recognizing that Śrīla Prabhupāda is making Kṛṣṇa and Vṛīndavana known all over the world, the Vṛīndavana city fathers have renamed the main road entering Vṛīndavana from Delhi “Bhaktivedanta Swami Marg” in his honor.

In Śrīdhama Māyāpur, the birthplace of Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu, the already completed guesthouse, temple and residence buildings mark the initial stages of what is planned to be an entire Kṛṣṇa conscious city. When completed, “The Vedic Planetarium and Temple of Understanding,” in the center of the city, will be the largest temple and cultural center in India.

UNDOUBTEDLY Śrīla Prabhupāda’s greatest contribution to the spiritual welfare of humanity are his books—nearly seventy volumes of commentaries upon and translations and summary studies of India’s immortal Vedic scriptures. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books have now been translated into twenty-six different languages.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books are unique because they present the message of the Vedic literature *as it is,* without personally motivated interpretatons. Thus, for the first time, the true glory of Vedic knowledge and culture is being recognized throughout the world.

More than fifty-five million copies of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books are now in print. The English edition of *Bhagavad-gitā As It Is,* now in its third printing, has already surpassed two and one-quarter million copies.

More than twenty-five hundred college professors and university and public libraries have placed standing orders for full sets of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books, twelve hundred of these orders from India alone. More than ninety percent of all the college an university libraries in the United States have ordered Śrila Prabhupāda’s books.

The spiritual benefit that accrues to the reader from Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books cannot even be estimated. But the appreciation that has poured in form the scholarly world can be at least partially understood from the following sample of reviews.

“Scholarship and religious devotion are joined in this work on the life of Caitanya....The present work is an monumental, seventeen volume translation of and commentary on Śri Caitanya-caritāmrta....

While many of the comments are short, some are fairly long (three to seven pages) and could stand alone as essays. The description of the abode of Kṛṣṇa, Vaikuṇṭha, is a beautifully written essay....The English syle is excellent....The set will be a valuable addition to upper division undergraduate and graduate libraries.”

̮̮̮April 1977 Choice Magazine  
(World’s Leading Academic Librarians Journal)

“I have now read considerable parts \[of *Bhagavad-gitā As It Is*\]. As a human being I cannot but applaud the fact that your movement is bringing faith and joy back into the lives of many unhappy young people who have not been able to find those virtues in the religion and culture in which they were raised, and I wish you success in the good work you are doing.”

Yours sincerely,  
A.L. Basham  
Professor and Head of the Department of Asian Civilizations  
The Australian National University

“We are well aware of the scholarly reputation of the works on Vedic and Bhaktic literature published by the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust, particularly the translation and commentaries, and feel that they meet, without doubt, the requirements of our Academy library, which have to do, above all, with authenticity. Our students are all deeply involved in comparative philosophical and religious studies, and have need of works which are not only of the highest scholarly standards, but also represent the traditions themselves, rather than only scholarly opinion about traditions. From this point of view, we...find the Bhaktivedanta publications of great value.”

Peter Lamborn Wilson, Librarian  
Imperial Iranian Academy of Philosophy

“The appearance of an English translaion of Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī’s *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmrta* by A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami is a cause for celebration among both scholars in Indian studies and lay people seeking to enrich their knowledge of Indian spirtuality...it will fill a most serious lacuna in our libraries and in our courses of the religious traditions of India.

“For the first time we possess a readily accessible edition of this great religious classic that will provide opportunity for scholars in Indian literature to compare the original text with a modern English translation and become acquainted with the deeper spiritual meanings of this work through the learned commentary of Śrī Bhaktivedanta.

“Anyone who bothers to give a close reading to the commentary will sense that here, as in his other works, Śrī Bhaktivedanta has combined a healthy mixture of the fervent devotion and aesthetic sensitivity of a devotee and the intelluctual rigou of a textual scholar. At no point does he allow the intended meaning of the text to be eclipsed by the promotion of a particular doctrinal persuasion.

“This exquisitely wrought volume will be a welcome edition to the library of all persons who are committed to the study of Indian spirituality and religious literature, whether their interests are sparked by the motivations of the scholar, the devotee or the general reader.”

Dr. J. Bruce Long  
Professor of Asian Studies  
Cornell University

“It has been a great pleasure to read over *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* which is very elaborate, very precise, and very succint. *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* by Swami Prabhupāda is an excellent presentation to the non-Sanskritic English-knowing population of the entire world. Problems dealing with Sanskritic works are manyfold. First of all, the linguistic peculiarities of Sanskrit make the students feel frustrated. Secondly comes the ethnic or cultural background which is solely responsible for the selection of exact semantic content. Thirdly, the context in which the given *śloka* is to be comprehended. Swami Prabhupādaji has solved all these problems simultaneously in this treatise by providing the transliteration, the world-translation, the free-translation, and the purport of every stanza. This book is extremely useful for those who are interested in History, Philosophy, Politics, Linuistics, Religion, Sociology, etc. of ancient India. I highly recommend this book for all the public libraries. Also this can be used as an undergraduate text for certain courses on Philosophy and or Religion of India. I wish Swami Prabhupādaji will bring forth treatises like this for the entire *Bhāgavatam*as well as other eminent Sanskrit works. Undoubtedly, this work of Swamijī’s is a great contribution to the troubled human society of today’s world.”

Dr. Sooda L. Bhatt  
Assistant Professor  
Boston University  
Boston, Mass.

“Of all the commentaries on *Bhagavad-gītā* that have appeared until now in the Western countries, this present edition by Śrī A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda is the best. In order to appreciate his success in presenting to the West the authorized edition of *Bhagavad-gītā* it is necessary to understand the significance or importance of Lord Kṛṣṇa, the original author. In the Vedic literature of India, Viśnu is considered to be the Supreme Being by whose power there is order and stability in nature and who is worshipped with a spirit of love and devotion, in other words, *bhakti,* and it is understood that from time to time He incarnates, either totally or partially, with the aim of helping humanity. Lord Kṛṣṇa is the original and complete form of Viśnu. I am very excited to see this publication in Spanish of *Bhagavad-gitā As It Is* by Śrī A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Śrīla Prabhupāda was brought up from his very birth in the strict practice of *bhakti-yoga* and he descends from a line of *gurus* which goes back to the original speaking of *Bhagavad-gītā* by Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Here Śrīla Prabhupāda’s knowledge of Sanskrit is surprising and without parallel. His penetration into the meaning of the text only corresponds to a fully illuminated or fully realized soul who has in fact understood perfectly the meaning of *Bhagavad-gītā.* I personally intend to use it, this book, in the courses that I direct or teach on the language, culture and philosophy of India or those which I am teaching by invitation of the Mexican Government. This authorized edition of *Bhagavad-gītā* will serve a double purpose in the countries of Spanish language: 1. It will help to stop the terrible cheating of *gurus* and *yogīs* who are false and unauthorized and 2. it will be an opportunity for the people who speak Spanish to understand the precise meaning or significance of Oriental culture.”

Dr. Kailas Vajpeye  
Dept. Oriental Studies  
University College of Mexico  
Mexico City

“A strange feature of the modern world is that in spite of vast advance in science and technology and the establishment of a good number of institutions for human welfare, mankind has not found tru peace and happiness. Knowledge of material sciences and arts has increased tremendously in recent times and millions of volumes on each fill the libraries the world over. People and leaders in every country are generally well-versed in these arts and sciences, but despite their efforts human society everywhere continues to be in turmoil and distress.

“The reason is not far to seek. It is that they have not learnt the Science of God, the most fundamental of every other art and science, and cared to apply it to the facts of life. The need of the hour is therefore to do it, if mankind is not only to survive but flower into a glorious existence.

“To teach this Science of God to people everywhere and to aid them in their progress and development towards the real goal of life, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is the most eminently fitted. In fact, this great and ancient work of Vyāsa will find this need of the modern times, for ‘it is a cultural presentation for the re-spiritualization of the entire human society.’

“His Divine Grace Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, the Founder-Ācārya of the ISKCON movement has taken upon himself, in addition to his ceaseless travels and other multifarious activities in the service of the Lord, the stupendous task of translating this Sanskrit work into English in about sixty volumes, for the welfare and happiness of mankind, It is really astonishing how he is able to do this single-handed, and when one comes to think of this, apart from his other great literary works, one is tempted to wonder if he is not the same Vyāsa Muni reborn today to adapt his own old work into a universal language of this age for the spiritual uplift of the modern man.

“So far, eight volumes of this most beautiful literature on God have been brought out by the ISKCON, and the rest are under preparation. Needless to say that in keeping with the excellence of their other publications, the publishers have seen to it that the printing, get-up and pictures in these volumes are also of the highest quality, as though to serve as an ornament to the divine contents of the books.

“This is a rare opportunity to people and leaders of every country, race and community in the world to know and understand the glorious Science of God and work for its perfection. I would say that this encyclopedia of spiritual knowledge is more important and fundamental than the encyclopedia of any other branch of knowledge and should, therefore, find a rightful place not only in the public and private libraries, big and small, of educational and oter institutions as also of every household, but above all in the hearts and minds of every man and woman.”

Śrī R. Subrahmanyam, M.A.  
Deputy Director (Research)  
Lok Sabha Secrtariat (National Parliament)  
New Delhi

“It is a great honor for me to have the pleasure of examining the publications of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust. I find all of them to be of exceptional value for use in educational institutions and libraries. Particularly the ancient classic *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* I recommend to all students and readers in Indian philosophy and culture. The original text is presented, along with roman transliteration, word-for-word synonyms, accurate translation and extremely lucid commentary and exposition. It is a remarkable work, and definitely a landmark in the matter of elevating humanity toward world unification and divine consciousness.

“The learned author, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, is a world-renowned saint and savant in the field of Vedic philosophy and its practical application in the modern world, where he has opened over a hundred spiritual *āśramas* for the study and cultivation of Vedic knowledge. Factually, he is unequalled in his efforts to establish the Vedic way of life, *sanātana-dharma,* in all countries of the world.

“Certainly I am most grateful that this message of the *Bhāgavata* is being spread for the benefit of the world by such a qualified personality as Swami Bhaktivedanta.”

Dr. Kalia, President  
Indian Library Association

“I consider as one of my greatest spiritual thrills that I had through a physical *darśana* (look) of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda on the 30th of March 1976 for the first time.

“I had the rare privilege to see with my own eyes the very embodiment of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the be-all and end-all of all beings.

“Śrī Swami Prabhupāda has established the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness, which is spread in the 108 cities around the world. All the members of ISKCON are pure *brāhmaṇas* and Vaiṣṇavas, initiated in the most austere *sādhana* recommended by the greatest Gosvāmis of Vṛndāvana, Śrī Rūpa Gosvāmi, the author of *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* and Śrī Sanātana Gosvāmi, the author of *Hari-bhakti-vilāsa* and many other works.

“I had earlier known Śrīla Prabhupāda through his enormous works on *kṛṣṇā-bhakti*, the nucleus of all Indian Vedic spiritual literature. To name a few of them, the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is, The Nectar of Devotion, Kṛṣṇa* (3 vols.), *Teachings of Lord Caitanya,* the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Śrī Caitanya-caritāmrta* and the commentary on *Śrī Īśopaniṣad* will suffice.

“It is noteworthy to mention that the *Īśopaniṣad Bhāśya* is the greatest contribution of Śrīla Prabhupāda as it completely refutes the Māyāvāda arguments of Śaṇkarācārya. This book is a treasure to be possessed by every Western scholar.

“Words fail me to describe the height of scholarship and devotion manifested in the volumes mentioned above. Our future generations and the posterity will definitely find a better world to live in through the efforts of Śrīla Prabhupāda as he stands for the feelings of International Brotherhood and spiritual integration of all mankind which is the back-bone of the Vedic *sanātana-dharma* of India. The literary world outside India, particularly the Westerners, should be highly indebted to him, who has so scientifically acquainted them with what is best in Kṛṣṇa conscious India.

“I may be permitted to recommend to all scholars, professors and other Dharmācāryas the great importance and contribution of the books of Śrī Swami Prabhupāda.”

Śrī Viswanath Sukla, Ph.D.  
Reader, Dept. Of Hindi. M.U.

“The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*is widely acclaimed as the Bible of Devotion *par excellence.* His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who has been the messenger of the Gospel of God-devotion abroad, has brought out an exquisite edition of the *Bhāgavatam* with an English translation and explanation. The translator brings the spirit of the text, and the explanation is lucid. Going through the Third Canto, part four, which the Publishers have sent me, I find that the reader can get the utmost benefit from this publication. The perfume of divine love exudes from every page. The format, the get-up, the illustrations, are all pleasing to the eye as well as to the mind.”

Dr. T.M.P. Mahadevan  
Centre of Advanced Study of Philosophy  
University of Madras  
Madras

“The *Bhagavad-gītā* is no doubt the most important and the best known work in the whole of the Indian literature. The magnificence of its spiritual concepts and the sublimity of its thinking has secured it a great popularity everywhere, as an easily intelligible presentation of the Divine. The *Gītā* has been edited, translated and commented countless times. The meaning of the text is, at least in its main lines, obvious and clear. The justification of a new interpretation is therefore, in my opinion, dependent on the message conveyed by the commentary.

“The translator and commentator Swami Bhaktivedanta represents the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava School and thus interprets the message of the *Gītā* ‘from the inside.’ Whole Śakara in Canto 5.12 interprets the plurality of the beings enumerated to be only conventional, and according to the Māyāvādīs the individuals after liberation merge into the impersonal Brahman, Swami Bhaktivedanta states that Kṛṣṇa here authoritatively emphasizes the eternity of the individuality. The transcendental form of God can be immediately experienced by a person who is duly prepared, as it is told in Canto 11. Just these two points are, I think, the reason for the interest in the *Gītā* among persons with a searching spirit. Swami Bhaktivedanta’s translation and commentary do deliver this message very convincingly indeed.”

Pentti Aalto  
Dept. of Asian and African Studies  
University of Helsinki  
Helsinki, Finland

REVIEWING the multitude of gifts that Śrīla Prabhupāda has given to us-his disciples-and to the world, we must conclude that no one has done more to glorify India, no one has done more to glorify Vedic culture, and no one has done more to glorify Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa in the modern age than our divine master, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

We offer our respectful obeisances millions of times at his lotus feet.

## Vyāsa-Pūjā homages from the G.B.C.

## Atreya Ṛṣi dāsa adhikāri, GBC-Middle East

Each new *śloka* of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* adds endlessly to my distress, knowing that the completion of this monumental task will bring your divine mission to its close, and leave me a hopeless, stubborn, wretched and fallen orphan. Soon you will no longer be there, in New York, Tehran or Māyāppur, to interject your causeless mercy into my darkened days. I fear that day when my parched dry heart will no longer receive your loving, compassionate showers, which are beyond all reason, logic and cause.

Due to this thought, a dark black cloud surrounds my whole existence and sinks me into a pit of abysmal depression. My whole life becomes meaningless, and the will to continue forsakes me. It is only then that the reality of this painful concern forces me to seek solace in the knowledge and realization you have so generously given me. A ray of hope enters my heart, and the voice of intelligence speaks thus to my weakened will: “Your Father has entrusted upon you Matchless Gifts, which will safeguard you and even lead you back to your eternal abode.”

As a neophyte I see your gifts as your words and your movement. Your words consist of philosophy, claims and instructions.

Your PHILOSOPHY is simple, yet it includes all the past *ācāryas,* religions and philosophies. It goes like this: Śrī Kṛṣṇa is the ultimate goal and the undiscovered pleasure in every object of desire. He is Govinda, the pleasure giver.

You say:

“She can not be that ultimate.  
This can not reach that ultimate.  
He can not have that ultimate.  
Kṛṣṇa says that He is the ultimate in everything.”  
It can not give that ultimate.  
That can not bring that ultimate.

Your CLAIM is very serious: Only those who follow YOUR instructions will reach that ultimate.

And your INSTRUCTIONS are the most straightforward. They cover every move, every act and every thought. Without doubt, with full authority and gravity, you repeatedly tell us: Together serve Kṛṣṇa unceasingly.

And this is the birth of your MOVEMENT-ISKCON. It is a precious gift, a golden carriage to carry on your words. It is your divine body, it is devotional service, it is Kṛṣṇa personified in this day on this planet. It is the path, the gate, and the home. In it you and your words will live eternally, and by it every living entity can reach his eternal abode.

Your final word to all of us is: simply cooperate, stick together, serve together. Particularly those of us you have entrusted with responsibility for managing (among whom I am one) should realize the full, practical meaning of cooperation and be faithful to your words in order to lead and teach leadership to others.

I see us far from this realization, but due to your mercy I have some faith. Faith in the spiritual miracles happening each day, in the consciousness of my Godbrothers, and that of my own. I also realize that I have to begin from myself, here and now, at the lotus feet of my Godbrothers.

Prabhupāda, rest assured, you have told us everything! It is now our duty to act and move towards that ultimate that you have promised us. You have requested that we cooperate, assuring that each of us can be a pure devotee.

If Kṛṣṇa is the ultimate and our goal is Kṛṣṇa, in order to reach that ultimate we reach out to you and beg your mercy on us fallen souls. You are our ultimate, we hold to your *dhoti,* take us Prabhupāda, take us!

*Your most fallen servant, on these pages by your infinite mercy,*

Atreya Ṛṣi dāsa adhikāri  
GBC-Middle East

## Balavanta dāsa adhikāri, GBC Southern U.S.A.

**Vyāsa-pūjā Homage to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda**

The spiritual master is the mercy incarnation of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. His message is the message of God. His desire is the desire of God, and his pleasure is the pleasure of God. There is no other way to approach Kṛṣṇa than through the medium of His beloved servant, the spiritual master. Therefore, we must fall at his feet in all humility, without personal prestige or motivation, and beg his merciful glance upon us. Without becoming his unalloyed and thoroughly devoted servants, there is absolutely no escape from the miserable conditions of material existence. We must be, therefore, ever vigilant not to mistake him to be an ordinary man of whom we might become envious. Rather, we should be always proud of our spiritual master—proud of his courage, proud of his wisdom, and proud of his purity. He is actually Kṛṣṇa, manifested before our crippled vision, because he is delivering the message of Kṛṣṇa. He speaks what Kṛṣṇa speaks, and he lives it for all to see.

The spiritual master remains always in Vṛndāvana because wherever he goes, he makes that place as good as Vṛndāvana. Internally, he relishes in Kṛṣṇa’s sublime pastimes of love, while externally he preaches the mission of Lord Caitanya for the benefit of all. Thus he is the best of all *jiva* souls and none is more dear to Kṛṣṇa than he.

Caught in *māyā’s* grip, we can only sit humbly at his lotus feet, eagerly drinking every sound that springs from his lotus lips. Then, with all enthusiasm, obedience and submission, we must execute his divine command. He is our only hope, our shelter in a hostile ocean, our oasis in a barren desert.

When we approach the spiritual master, we must cast aside the crutches of material designations. We cannot maintain such artificialities and surrender utterly at his lotus feet. Foolish pride and a false sense of independence veil our real knowledge and hide the spiritual master from our vision. To become the menial servants of the spiritual master constitutes the perfection of our existence. Then our spiritual life is awakened, our eternal *rasa* established, and our platform of enjoyment revived. Once surrendered we are not disturbed, in any condition nor do we have any material desires, being completely satisfied simply to be his faithful servants. Unable to attain this position, the unsurrendered disciple, Balavanta dāsa, continues to suffer without solace and can only lament his wretched condition, praying for the benediction rain of his spiritual master’s compassion.

Balavanta dāsa adhikāri  
GBC Southern U.S.A.

## Bhagavān dāsa adhikāri, GBC-Southern Europe

With the passing of each day, the vision of the newborn child expands. At first everything is unclear; then vague forms appear. Soon he is able to recognize that there are special people who always are concerned with his welfare. In this way he comes to know his father and mother as his security and protection. O beloved Gurudeva, you have taken us under your protection as your own children. At first we could not see what your exalted position was. But you gently opened our eyes with your divine knowledge and love, and soon it became clear that you were our eternal father, master and protector. As we watch your books appear, like golden waves emanating from your lotus mouth, you establish within our hearts the irrevocable faith that you are the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead. Merciful Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am such a low fool that I do not even qualify to look upon your lotus feet. Still you are so kind that you have personally instructed me on so many different occasion-even in the privacy of your personal quarters.

I pray to Śrī-Śrī Gaura-Nitāi that my mind be always overflowing with your divine instructions and that I become more and more attached to serving your lotus feet, as a person is attached to his very breath.

Bhagavān dāsa adhikāri  
GBC-Southern Europe

## Brahmānanda Svāmi, GBC-Africa

The disciple’s perfection’s when the guru is pleased.  
I’m wanting that goal, and your orders received  
Are a boon that makes me the most fortunate soul.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, a Kṛṣṇa conscious Africa’s your goal.  
You’ve made it so easy, you’ve done everything first.  
You’ve cut a clear path under conditions much worse.  
To America you came penniless and alone.  
Now you’re captain of the ship that will take us all home.  
You sent me on a voyage that seemed like years.  
Twelve days, just your words made me lose all fears.  
Step by step you guide us, the footing is safe.  
But I’m so hard-hearted, I forget and lose faith.  
And as the failing pupil gets the teacher’s special aid,  
A Nairobi visit by your mercy was paid.  
Black students to the walls, your preaching to hear,  
The biggest crowd assembled in more than a year,  
Your form was effulgent and your message divine,  
“Please chant Hare Kṛṣṇa for a life so sublime.”  
The first step was taken, it was clear what to do,  
You’re the Founder-Ācārya, so we just followed you.  
Chanting and dancing in Thompkins Square Park  
Now happened again. Local papers remarked:  
“When you see these people, don’t say ’Jambo!’  
Say ‘Hare Kṛṣṇa!’ It’s much better than ‘Hello!’  
Our programs were famous at the housing estates.  
No one could guess how much prasāda they ate.  
Our films were a hit, and Lord Jagannātha’s smile  
Brought a great cheer and clapping heard for thousands of miles.  
The Safari kīrtana was the best it could be,  
Street dancing, prasāda and books that were free.  
One evening began with a Benz caravan.  
Indian members assembled, a sedate program they’d planned.  
Then we opened the doors and much to our surprise  
An ecstatic swirling mass of black kids arrived.  
The kīrtana began, and the sound was so ecstatic  
That I jumped off stage and began dancing automatic.  
“Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Rāma!” was their only reply.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, you’ve made Africa the Spiritual Sky.  
Our programs are small now, as it is hard to maintain.  
Many blacks and whites came but few have remained.  
The potential still here though, our “Club” does quite well.  
Eighty men come twice daily, take prasāda and then yell: “Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare  
Rāma!” and “Jaya Jagannātha!”  
It’s working Śrīla Prabhupāda, it breaks that hard knot.  
A few African men are now holding on to your feet,  
And it brings back the times that have now become sweet.  
How you took me by the hand and so extra patiently  
Brought me up from the gutter to learn A B C.

*kirāta-hūṇāndhra-pulinda-pulkaśā*  
*ābhīra-śumbhā yavanāḥ khasādayaḥ*  
*ye 'nye ca pāpā yad-apāśrayāśrayāḥ*  
*śudhyanti tasmai prabhaviṣṇave namaḥ*  
*(Bhāg. 2.4.18)*

Kirāta, Hūna, Āndhra, Pulinda,  
Pulkaśa, Ābhīra, Śumbha, Yavana,  
The Khasa and others addicted to sin  
Are purified by the shelter of the devotees of Him.  
The Lord’s the Supreme Power, and I beg to be found  
With my head on the spot where His feet touch the ground.  
You said this temple’s real name was Kirāta-śuddhi.  
Now five years have passed, and I’m beginning to see  
The Africans are fallen aboriginal men,  
But the purification is needed in myself more than them.  
Vaiśṇavas have a system or cleaning the heart,  
And that system takes effect as soon as you start.  
No bars on the nation or the colour of skin.  
A Vaiśṇava’s strong enough to make changes within.  
Strī, śūdra, kirāta or dvija-bandhu.  
O Prabhupāda, why can’t we just do what you do?  
Kṛṣṇa’s friends are so busy cleaning dust-worn minds,  
But my hours are spent with the faults that I find.  
You say it’s so easy, “Hold this Kṛṣṇa science fast,  
Become the Lord’s man and reclaim the general mass.”  
Śrī Caitanya’s order is to travel the globe  
Reclaiming all persons who want to be told.  
A reasonable man with an inquisitive mind  
Can accept this great message of a life so sublime.  
Vaiśṇāvas don’t all come from one special land.  
Just as you, Gurudeva, aren’t an ordinary man.  
By the service of the Lord and your spiritual guide,  
A special change happens within and outside.  
A Vaiśṇava’s body changes spiritually at once.  
Anyone who thinks different is clearly a dunce.  
Lord Caitanya’s all-powerful, and under any circumstance,  
anyone from anywhere can take prasāda and dance.  
His self-declaration teaches how we should be:  
“I’m the servant of the servant of Lord Gopināthji!”  
The devotees are few here, and perhaps it’s because  
The news about Africa’s gossiping buzz.  
Even though they are few, they’re first-class by your grace.  
They’re enthused more than me for preaching in this dark place.  
I thank you for sending them to help me.  
When I see them it’s only your mercy I see.  
We’re now just maintaining, but that doesn’t please you.  
You said expand here and these programs renew:  
Ratha-yātrā, a farm, and Gaura-Nitāi on parikrama,  
Go to every town and village and distribute the bombs.  
The Name and prasāda to each and every man.  
I’ll work until Africa’s called “Black Vṛṇdāvana.”  
Now I’m thinking to do this so as not to speak lame,  
But one problem is here and it’s me whose to blame.  
There’s a great difference between us, Prabhupāda, you and me.  
When you came to America you made your army.  
I’m not pure, so that feat is a bit out of sight.  
I depend on the men you send for my might.  
I want to please you and fulfil your desire,  
But without first-class men we’ll not kindle that fire.  
Some men are quite pure now, so let them come here.  
They’ll be stronger than ever if they serve for two years.  
The work is not hard, and yet spiritually  
There’s a test every minute that when taken blissfully  
Trains the mind to take shelter like the buzzing honeybee.  
Many men aren’t needed; one akśauhiṇī will do  
Or just one mahā-ratha, Śrīla Prabhupāda, like you.  
To please you is the perfection of every devotee.  
Whether preaching or farming or vaidhi-bhakti.  
Their services vary, but one thing’s the same-  
Your pleasure is the highest goal to attain.  
The Supreme Lord’s all-powerful: prabhaviśṇave namaḥ.  
He’ll accept all our service even though He’s so far.  
So I beg for His blessing and His sanctioning will  
That your orders sublime I’ll completely fulfil.  
If this can be done, there’s no doubt in my heart,  
A journey back home I’ll be able to start.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving me a chance  
To serve you and take prasāda and dance.

(versified by Jānakīnātha dāsa brahmacārī)

Brahmānanda Svāmi  
GBC-Africa

## Gopāla Kṛṣṇa dāsa adhikārī, GBC-Northern and Western India, BBT Trustee

I beg to offer my most respectful *daṇḍavats* unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master, Oṁ Viśṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aśṭottara-śata Śrī-Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. You are the genuine representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva, and therefore entitled to receive offerings from your disciples every year. You are the most faithful and confidential servitor of the Lord and therefore deserve as much respect as Lord Kṛṣṇa. In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* it is said that although every living entity is a servitor of God, the genuine spiritual master is a direct manifestation of the Supreme Lord. For a disciple to advance spiritually, he must be convinced of this.

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* Lord Kṛṣṇa tells Uddhava:

*ācāryaṁ mām vijānīyān nāvamayeta karhicit*  
*na martya-buddyāsūyeta sarva-devamāya guruḥ*

**“One should know the** ***ācārya*** **as Myself and never disrespect him in any way. One should not envy him, thinking him an ordinary man, for he is the representative of all the demigods.” (*****Bhāg.*** **11.17.27*****)***

Here Lord Kṛṣṇa specially instructing how a disciple should behave under the care of his spiritual master. A spiritual master is not an enjoyer of facilities offered by his disciples; rather, he is like a parent. Just like a child cannot grow up without the attentive guidance of his parents, one cannot learn to perform devotional service without the attentive guidance of the spiritual master. The ability to walk is born with a baby, and within a couple of years the child develops it with practice. Similarly, Kṛṣṇa consciousness is eternally present within the conditioned soul but can only be realized when guided by a true representative of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

The Supreme Lord is so kind that He appears in two features to save the conditioned soul—externally as the *ācārya* and internally as the Supersoul. You have appeared just to deliver the fallen *mlecchas.* Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda used to say, “If at all there is a shortage of anything, it is of love of God.” The modern *rākśasa* leaders are denying God’s existence and thus misleading the human population. You took your spiritual master’s instruction to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness seriously, and therefore you are so successful. It is essential that a disciple follow his spiritual master’s instructions rigidly. You are a true *tattva-darśī,* or knower of the absolute truth, and therefore always anxious to preach. Even in your present illness, I see that you do not miss any opportunity to talk of Lord Kṛṣṇa. You are a great Vaiśṇāva, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You are always trying to save the suffering humanity by giving them the supreme knowledge. You will always live by your divine instructions. On this auspicious day, I pray that I will always follow your instructions and help spread Lord Caitanya’s message. Only this will please you; nothing else will.

*Your eternal servant,*

Gopāla Kṛṣṇa dāsa adhikārī  
GBC-Northern and Western India  
BBT Trustee

## Hanṣadūta Svāmi, GBC-Śrī Laṇkā

*My Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I cannot write anything that will properly glorify you. I am a condemned sinful creature. Somehow I was born with two hands and legs and have adopted the dress of a devotee as your disciple, but there is not a minuscular particle of sincerity or understanding in my heart.

I wonder how it is that I have passed so many empty days in your movement, surrounded by hundreds and thousands of your pure devotees without serving them properly, and still be allowed to continue. Your mercy is surely great. The mercy of your devotees is carrying me along back home, back to Godhead, although I am completely worthless and disqualified.

I cannot ask to be picked up as one of the most fallen in this world because you have already done that by your mere glance. I like to think that I might one day be accepted by your devotees as one of their devotees.

My words are empty and pretentious, but I continue to write this offering because I have no other alternative than to go through the motions and expressions of a devotee, hoping, like an alchemist trying to turn iron into gold, that Your Divine Grace will fill my empty words with spiritual life.

Dear Prabhupāda, no one knows who you really are and where your will go when your work on earth is done. How will I be able to keep hold of your lotus feet and remain free from the attraction of material energy which bewilders even Brahmā, the engineer of this universe? Therefore I think that you must make some special arrangement for me, because I am completely helpless and dependent upon you.

Your humble servant,

*Hanṣadūta Svāmi*  
*GBC-Śrī Laṇkā*

## Harikesa Svāmi, BBT trustee

Some people preach but do not act very nicely, while others act very nicely but do not preach. The wonder of the pure devotee (known as the *ācārya*) is that he both acts nicely and preaches. We have practically seen that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, both act in wonderful ways and preach perfectly according to time, place, and circumstances.

You have conquered over the urge to speak, the urges of the mind, the force of anger and the forces of the tongue, belly, and genital. You have no desire for sense gratification and are free from all varieties of bodily conceptions. Therefore you are the perfect gosvāmi. Although now your body has grown “old and weak,” you are pushing it on through the force of your spiritual potency in order to spread this movement all over the world.. This is simply amazing. (By this example we are forced to understand that dead matter is forced to work by the energy of the spirit soul.) any other man would have lain down and died long ago, but you are expanding your service all the time. Although the demons are becoming more and more troublesome and place all kinds of obstacles in our path, you never become discouraged; rather, you inspire us more by your strong words and superhuman feats.

You are never disturbed at any moment because you are fixed in the Supreme, despite all difficulties which may arise due to the body, the demigods or other living beings. Because you are always united with the supreme will of the Lord, your activities are perfect in every way. We cannot understand how you constantly think of saving the fallen souls even when we disciples are ready to kill them for their being so foolish and demoniac and causing the devotees so much trouble. Therefore you are the *ācārya*. Prahlāda Mahārāja said:

*naivodvije para duratyaya-vaitaraṇyās*  
*tvad-vīrya-gāyana-mahāmṛta-magna-cittaḥ*  
*śoce tato vimukha-cetasa indriyārtha-*  
*māyā-sukhāya bharam udvahato vimūḍhān*  
*(SB 7.9.43)*

**“O best of the great personalities, I am not at all afraid of material existence, for wherever I stay I am fully absorbed in thoughts of Your glories and activities. My concern is only for the fools and rascals who are making elaborate plans for material happiness and maintaining their families, societies and countries. I am simply concerned with love for them.”**

Once I asked you when you were reading the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, Seventh Canto, “Prabhupāda, I think you are always enwrapped in the mood of Prahlāda Mahārāja.” You said, “Yes—I am always thinking of how to save all these fools and rascals.” You are always thinking how they might be relieved from their miserable conditions of existence and engaged in the service of the Lord. Although Prahlāda could have felt so much personal discomfort, extreme anxiety and pain by the persecutions of the demons, he was free from all difficulties because he simply merged in the ocean of transcendental bliss by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *maha-mantra*. You are always fully merged in the ocean of transcendental bliss by chanting and constantly meditating on how these fools and rascals can come to the highest perfection.

You have fully surrendered your life on the order of your spiritual master to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the Western world, and you are doing that wonderfully through your books. You are so kindly giving us all that which is necessary to regain our lost transcendental consciousness and enter into the kingdom of God. By your mercy we can know that the kingdom of God is here when we engage in chanting His holy names and preaching His glories to the fallen souls.

It is simply because of your books that the world atmosphere is becoming clearer and people can see things as they are. Even though they cannot understand the books (perhaps they are not even aware of their existence), which are so spiritually powerful, the effect is there and people are imperceptibly becoming purified although they cannot understand how or why. The completely demoniac trend of human society is gradually changing, and soon the time will come when Kṛṣṇa will arrange the ways and means that Kṛṣṇa consciousness will envelop the world in floods of transcendental ecstasy, and it is all because of your books.

So you are the perfect *ācārya*, acting and preaching in the most perfect way, guiding us (especially me—how fallen and sinful a person I am!) to the light of knowledge and gradually freeing us from the bondage of illusion. Your every activity is leading us back home, back to Godhead, and you are forcing all the conditioned souls to also return someday, although they have no idea to do it on their own.

I am praying that you will have me always as your humble servitor, and if ever I decide to deviate from the correct path of devotion, please beat me severely and drag me back to your lotus feet.

Your foolish servant,

*Harikesa Svāmi*  
*BBT trustee*  
*GBC for Eastern Europe, Germany, Scandinavia, Switzerland and Austria*

## Hṛdayānanda dāsa Gosvāmī, GBC-South America

**Śrīla Prabhupāda Pranati**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*Prema-bhakti yāhā haite, avidyā-vināśa yāte, vede gāya yāḥāra carita:*

O great spiritual master! O most glorious Śrīla Prabhupāda! The exquisite Vedic *mantras* sing ecstatically of your character. O most perfect Master! I fall down in the dust of your lotus feet, begging you to fan the spark of love within my heart. Kindle within my heart the blazing fire of unalloyed love for your lotus feet, so that the fire will immediately burn to ashes all fruitive desires and mental speculation.

*Śrī-guru-carae rati, ei sei uttama-gati, yei prasāde pūre sarva āśā:*

O most sublime messenger of Vaikuṇṭha! Your two lotus feet are the transparent via media for seeing the abode of Kṛṣṇa. All liberations are found in the dust of your feet. Gorgeous and eternal worlds are beheld from the vantage point of the dust of your lotus feet. By tasting the nectar of the dust of your feet, all material desires are quickly annihilated. I am groping in the darkness of lust, O Gurudeva! Lead me at once to the dust of your feet!

*Guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete kariyā aikya, āra nā kariha mane āśā:*

O most fascinating of those who speak and chant! Shine the beacon of your transcendental speaking on my heart. The blissful songs that you sing are a river of nectar flowing into our ears. All the infinite Vaikunṭha planets floating in the spiritual sky, as well as the limitless material creation, are all revealed in your Bhaktivedanta purports. Only you know our self-interest; let your instructions be eternally engraved in our minds and hearts. Give us strength to drive out of our minds the venomous black snakes of opinions opposing your instructions.

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

O great Gurudeva! You are glorious like the Supreme Personality of Godhead. *Sākśād dhartivena samasta-śāstraih:* O inconceivable servitor Godhead! You are so merciful that you permit a shameless rogue such as myself to participate in your earthly pastimes. In your glorious descent to the earth, you do not kill the demons such as myself, but you save us and become our spiritual master. O beloved Gurudeva, you have shown your mercy to me who was floundering in animal life. Now by your inconceivable mercy let that animal become human, and let that human become Vaiśnāva, and please, beloved Gurudeva, let that Vaiśnāva be permitted to humbly and obediently return to his original, eternal post as an atom at the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa.

Hṛdayānanda dāsa Gosvāmī  
GBC-South America
