## Bhaktivedanta Institute

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Real science means to approach a bona fide spiritual master and submissively inquire from him about the Absolute Truth in a spirit of sincere service. In this way one can learn the truth through practical realization in a systematic and effective way. Without spiritual knowledge, scientists, philosophers and scholars can only develop educational systems that basically amount to what has been called "intellectual animalism." This means that the result of their knowledge is directed ultimately toward the bodily necessities of eating, sleeping, mating and defending. These necessities, common to both men and animals, are met in the animal kingdom without universities. The human society should also take care of these necessities without great endeavor and thus free the human intellect for higher purposes.

*Athāto brahma-jijñāsā*, the motto of the Bhaktivedanta Institute, expresses this higher purpose perfectly. The human form of life is meant for inquiring into what I am, what God is, what the meaning of life and the goal of knowledge is. These are the questions of thoughtful men, and one must know the proper process for answering them—*tad viddhi praṇipātena*, by approaching the spiritual master.

Those who simply want to work hard for sense gratification cannot understand that life is a principle distinct from and superior to matter, and that the supreme life is Kṛṣṇa. All scientific, literary and philosophical research should be directed toward this conclusion, *idaṁ hi puṁsas tapasaḥ śrutasya vā sviṣṭasya sūktasya ca buddhi-dattayoh* \[SB 1.5.22\]. Once having cleared away all doubts by careful understanding that Kṛṣṇa is everything, *vāsudevaḥ sarvam iti*, then one should fully surrender unto Him, *sarva-dharmān parityajya*, engaging everything in His devotional service. In this way by fixing the mind on Kṛṣṇa in pure, unalloyed devotional service *man-manā bhava mad-bhaktaḥ*(*Bg.* 18.65), one can attain the supreme destination of life and go back home, back to Godhead, *yānti mad-yājino 'pi mām \[*Bg. 9.25\]. The practical achievement of this goal can be realized simply by carrying out the orders of the spiritual master, accepting him as the authorized representative of the Supreme Lord.

We are so fortunate to have the opportunity to learn from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Essential truth concisely spoken is true eloquence. In a few words you can eloquently point out that which thousands of years of human speculation have failed to discover. Thus you are the greatest scientist, the most perfect professor, the most learned scholar and our most beloved spiritual father. You are not concerned with the mundane wranglings of inert minds, but you fill our hearts with transcendental knowledge, *divya-jñana hṛde prakāśita*. Attachment to your lotus feet is the perfection that fulfills all desires. We can feel very secure in carrying out your instructions perfectly. You have mercifully taught us to abandon the grinding mill of arrogant scholarship and to take up the path of humble endeavor in devotional service as the path of knowledge. By your mercy only is our success assured.

## Bhuvaneśvara

Today is the one day set aside especially for the glorification of our beloved spiritual master, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Of course, every day we are worshiping His Divine Grace: *Śrī-guru-caraṇa-padma*. The lotus feet of our spiritual master are the only way by which we can attain pure devotional service. This is our daily prayer, but it is on this day, his glorious appearance day, that we stop and make a double appreciation of our lord and master, who so graciously bestowed his mercy on us by appearing on this day. Although every day in the association of a pure devotee is a festive occasion, the day on which he appears for giving us this daily association is doubly important and most dear to our hearts. He has appeared the day after the appearance day of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, which illustrates the ancient Vedic adage "by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa one gets guru"—now by the guru's mercy we shall be able to get Kṛṣṇa. *Yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto 'pi*. Here in this verse Viśvanātha Cakravatī Thākura emphasizes, as does Narottama dāsa Thākura in *Śrī-guru-caraṇa-padma*, the divine importance of having the transcendental spiritual guide. As the student without a teacher is a fool, the ship without a captain is sunk, the mountain climber without a guide is lost, so similarly the spiritual aspirant without the divine master is doomed to failure on the path to spiritual perfection.

Human life begins at the point when one accepts with faith and devotion a spiritual master for his guidance out of the darkness of ignorance. But who is to be chosen, and how, is of the utmost importance, for to choose an impostor as a guide on the path of transcendence is the paramount danger which one may meet on his sojourn through this material world; nay, it can practically mean his eternal damnation here. As the teacher without a degree cannot teach, as a captain cannot sail without thorough experience, as the mountain guide cannot lead without having gone the path himself before, similarly the spiritual master which one chooses must be full completely with the required qualifications to guide one back along the venerable but difficult path of transcendence. The first and foremost is that he is an *ācārya* in the bona fide line of disciplic succession. Therein all qualifications are contained. First we should examine the disciplic succession and what it means: It is the unbroken chain of spiritual masters who have handed down intact the divine message of pure devotional service as it was enunciated by the original spiritual master, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, to His first disciple, Lord Brahmā. There is never any addition or subtraction made. As it was received, so it was given—the transcendental message. It is out of divine love and faith for the Lord that the spiritual masters will never change the message of the Lord, lest their heart of hearts be drowned in a ocean of grief at disappointing their beloved. So the *ācārya*is one who through his thought, word and deeds teaches the message of the disciplic succession, as inculcated to him by the guru, the *śāstras*and *sādhus*—previous saints in the line, and it is this alone which makes one qualified to be the preceptor of the spiritual path.

Śrī Rūpa Gosvāmī, the eminent scholar and intimate disciple and associate of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and poet among poets in Vaiṣṇava literature, has deftly praised the qualities of an *ācārya*in his concise but powerful and sweet treatise the Upadeśāmṛta. In the first verse he enunciates:

*vāco vegaṁ manasaḥ krodha-vegaṁ*  
*jihvā-vegam udaropastha-vegam*  
*etān vegān yo viṣaheta dhīraḥ*  
*sarvām apīmāṁ pṛthivīṁ sa śiṣyāt*

*Vaco vegam*-It is most difficult to control the urge to speak. The tongue is most voracious and eager to speak always and anything, without cessation. It is he who can always control the ever-swaggering tongue and train it to vibrate transcendental sound only. Then he alone can become the *ācārya*.

*Manasaḥ krodha-vegam*-"My dear Kṛṣṇa," Arjuna entreats, "is it not more difficult to control the mind than to control the raging wind?" The mind is so stubborn and obstinate that it controls the intelligence at times, but he who trains the mind to concentrate always on the beautiful cowherd boy Govinda, His form, name, pastimes, paraphernalia and associates, he alone can be an *ācārya*. Did not Viśvāmitra the great sage become angry with Rambhā, did not Brahmā the creator of the universe become furious with the Kumāras, did not Lord Śiva, who is Mahādeva, burn Cupid to ashes in rage, but did not Lord Viṣṇu massage the feet of Bhṛgu upon the latter's insult? It is only when one is in the transcendental mode of pure goodness that one can be free of anger, because anger is the product of lust, and lust is the product of attachment via contemplation of the objects of the senses. So it is only one whose mind is fixed on the Supreme Lord, whose speech always glorifies the transcendental Lord, and whose gross senses, the tongue, the mind and the genital, are always withdrawn from the objects of the senses—completely controlled—he can renounce anger, the loss of which brings boundless joy. He alone can become an *ācārya*.

*Jihvā-vegam*- "The only way to a man's heart is through his stomach" is the old American quotation. The lusty desires of the cook are transferred through the food to the mind of the waiting recipient; the wicked desires of the hotel's cook are also readily available in the fancy prepared dishes. The heart is an open receptacle for the wicked and lusty desires of the cooks who prepare foodstuff's, which encourage sinful activities, save and except the foodstuffs prepared by the hands of the ardent devotee of the Lord for the exclusive pleasure of the Lord, the remnants of which are distributed and shared with great relish after being offered to the Lord by the devotee. One who can, with all the determination at his command, remove all desires for tasting anything but Śrī Kṛṣṇa's divine remnants can alone be called an *ācārya*.

*Udaropastha-vegam*-one should not endeavor to make the body fat and strong, thus increasing the bodily conception of life and clouding the path to spiritual perfection. When the body increases in weight and strength, the urge to enjoy the genital also increases. One who denies the body undue and excessive increase in the vital fluid of life and conserves it therein for memory, determination and enthusiasm for spiritual pursuits, accepting only enough to keep body and soul together, he alone can be an *ācārya*.

The prince among poets in Vaiṣṇava literature, Śrī Rūpa Gosvāmī, says that *etān vegān*—he who controls all these urges—is *viṣaheta dhīraḥ*, the one and only *dhīra*, a sober person in this world. Undisturbed by the influence of the modes of material nature on these six sense gratifying agents, he has conquered the material nature. The *dhīra*is guru, or heavy; that is, he is situated in one place above the material ocean's wave. Therefore Śrī Rūpa says, *sarvām apīmām pṛthivīṁ sa śiṣyat*—he alone can go throughout the world and make disciples because he alone has become *ācārya*, one who teaches the path of transcendence from the guru, the *śāstra*, and the *sādhus*, by his thought, word, and deeds.

The *ācārya*is a veritable transcendental repository of spiritual knowledge and thoughts. His association is like touchstone, enlightening everyone he meets via the touch of transcendental sound vibration, which enters and purifies the heart of every true seeker who practically sees with his own eyes that every action of the *ācārya*is a manifestation of his divine knowledge and teachings, an example for all to follow. Such a veritable king should be approached with great caution, for Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself has said, *ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyāt*. The *ācārya*is My very self. Just as the ambassador is given equal reception as the president he represents, so the spiritual master is to be received like Kṛṣṇa because he is thinking, speaking and acting on behalf of the Lord. He is doing what the Lord Himself would do for us if we were able to see Him with our spiritually blind eyes. We cannot reach the Lord in the heart, so He has come out and manifested Himself as guru, the external manifestation of Paramātmā, the Lord in the heart. And because He is in contact with Paramātmā, who is everywhere in full knowledge of everything, there is nothing which is secret for the *ācārya*. The *ācārya*may know whatever he may need or choose to know at any given second. As Vyāsa was *tri-kāla-jña*, or able to perceive time in all three phases—past, present and future—so the *ācārya*has this power. Therefore it is said, *sarva-devamayo guruh*: he is the sum total of all the demigods. If one worships the *ācārya*, then he has fulfilled all his commitments to all the demigods. The *ācārya* is a veritable repository of all strength, fame, knowledge, beauty, and wealth. He is unrivalled in the worlds and should be approached with only the utmost respect and veneration. Verily it is said that when one stands before the spiritual master he should think himself a total fool. Further it is stated that one must never be envious of the *ācārya*, for it is enviousness of Kṛṣṇa. If the ambassador is insulted, the president is insulted, therefore envy is an offense at the lotus feet of the spiritual master, which is known as Vaiṣṇava-*aparādha*. Such an offense causes havoc on the progressive path back to Godhead. One should strictly avoid it. The *ācārya* should be approached in a humble frame of mind (*paripraśnena sevayā*), with an innocent and inquisitive mind and a humble service attitude, because this is the only method and combination which endears the guru to enlighten the disciple with transcendental knowledge. It is this knowledge we must obtain to transcend the material world.

No one is more kind and gracious than the *ācārya*. Coming to this rotten, miserable material world! What a great sacrifice for all of us, who are so unworthy—suffering for our sinful reactions, reducing our burden in traversing the path to spiritual progress. He is the greatest servant to all humanity, for he is the greatest servant of the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Therefore he is master, spiritual master, *ācārya*, and it is his path we must follow, holding on tightly to his *dhoti*, for it is by his merciful glance alone that we can go back to Godhead. He has spared no pains in fulfilling the orders of his guru-mahārāja in coming to the Western world, facing terrible conditions of health, weather, eating, sleeping and traveling, giving up all bodily comforts, undaunted in his determination that "I must satisfy my guru-mahārāja. "This consciousness we must also attain. This we must also cry out for. We must throw off all of our material desires, shun all hope for liberation, and pray constantly day and night with all the courage at our command that we can in some small way satisfy even minutely our beloved spiritual master, His Divine Grace.

A new day is dawning in the history of the world, the face of all nations will change, their culture, their eating habits, their sleeping habits, their processes of mating and defending, their routine of keeping clean, everything from A to Z is being changed—their thoughts, their words and their deeds—all by the grace of one single personality, a personality so divine, so powerful, so learned, so beautiful, so wealthy, so renounced, so famous that he defies comprehension by the greatest material intellect and the swiftest material mind. Let us all on this day renew our pledge to become an integral part in his plan of changing the entire destiny of mankind by unreservedly surrendering our every thought, word and deed at the lotus feet of our spiritual master, who Thākura Bhaktivinoda predicted would come and preach the message of Lord Caitanya unrestrictedly throughout the world. His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, the fulfiller of Lord Caitanya's prophecy that **"**My name shall be heard in every town and village of the world."

Gaura Govinda Svāmī / Dhīraśānia dāsa brahmacārī / Vyāpī dāsa brahmacārī / Bhagavat dāsa brahmacārī / Prakāśātmā dāsa brahmacārī / Hare Kṛṣṇa dāsa brahmacārī / Sañjāta dāsa brahmacārī / Premalauna dāsa brahmacārī / Digpati dāsa adhikārī / Jitaprāṇa dāsa brahmacārī / Mahābhuja dāsa brahmacārī

## Bloomington

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

We have no qualifications to make an offering to Your Divine Grace. Any qualification we may have to serve you is due to your causeless mercy only. So please let us realize our position, which is just like that of a sold-out animal, who is completely dependent upon its master in every respect. You are our master and have given us everything. You have brought us the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*, the medicine that will wipe out the disease of illusion from which we are suffering. You are maintaining us daily, supplying us with *kṛṣṇa-prasāda*, with the shelter of your Vaikuṇṭha temples and with the association of *kṛṣṇa-bhaktas*. You have placed us under the expert care of your devotee, His Holiness Kīrtanānanda Svāmī. You have given us Śrī Śrī Rādhā Vṛndāvana Candra to saturate our eyes and purify our hearts. You are destroying the long-cherished desire for sense gratification and in turn you are filling the mouths of your devotees with *kṛṣṇa-kathā*. You are a miracle worker, transforming dark-hearted *mlecchas*into bright-faced Vaiṣṇavas. And now it is coming to pass that in every town and village the holy names of Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Rāma are being vibrated because you have so faithfully followed the instructions of your guru-mahārāja. His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī. It is described that one drop of Lord Caitanya's mercy can inundate the entire world with pure love for Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are that one drop. Your Divine Grace is our only hope in this world of birth and death. O Gurudeva, please do not let us escape the deluge of your mercy.

Please help this sinner catch hold of your lotus feet. All glories, all glories, all glories to the emissary of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Briṣakāpi dāsa / Papasthāya dāsa / Sārjādi-devī dāsī / Nityodita dāsa / Kṛṣṇaṣtāmī dāsa / Varaha-devī dāsī

## Bogota

One who thinks that the spiritual master is a common man certainly possesses hellish intelligence and his eternal captivity in illusion is a certainty. Śrīla Prabhupāda, your body is completely spiritual and your every breath is a prayer to Kṛṣṇa. Your smile and shining teeth are a gateway to the spiritual world, and from your mouth there is a constant cascade of golden Vaikuṇṭha nectar. May I always bathe myself in the *kṛṣṇa-kathā*which flows from your lotus mouth. Your eyes are like a window to Goloka, and if one steadily gazes into your lotus eyes he becomes melted in ecstasy, for they twinkle with pure love of Godhead.

There are so many so-called important persons and foolish knaves in this material world who count themselves as greatly fortunate. But I know that I am truly blessed by Lord Caitanya because I have been allowed to serve you and become counted among your disciples. I do not care a fig for those so-called lucky fellows because I know that all their good luck goes sour at the bleak moment of their death. But among countless conditioned souls I have been benedicted by Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda's mercy, and so I, who am worse than worthless, have been allowed to serve you, who are the best of all persons. No one else is the *ācārya*, no one else is *jagad-guru*, and no one else is more dear to me than yourself. I pray that after many lifetimes of service. Lord Kṛṣṇa will allow me to become your devotee.

Your eternal servants, ISKCON, Bogota, Colombia

## Bombay

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda:*

Please accept our eternal prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace. On the auspicious occasion of Śrī Vyāsa-Pūjā we meditate on your unlimited transcendental qualities and glorious pastimes in reclaiming the fallen conditioned souls, and we find that you are the personification of the transcendental love described by Śrī Prahlāda Mahārāja.

*naivodvije para duratyaya-vaitaraṇyās*  
*tvad-vīrya-gāyana-mahāmṛta-magna-cittaḥ*  
*śoce tato vimukha-cetasa indriyārtha-*  
*māyā-sukhāya bharam udvahato vimūḍhān*  
*SB 7.9.43*

You were living alone in Vṛndāvana, absorbed in thoughts of the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa. From your sacred rooms at Rādhā Dāmodara, looking over the *bhajana-kuṭira*of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmi, you were feeling transcendental bliss. Merged in that ocean of *bhakti-rasa*, what need was there for you to leave that holy place? What need was there for you to leave the sanctified atmosphere of Vṛndāvana, so pure and peaceful, to come to the hellish cities of the West?

*prāyeṇa deva munayaḥ sva-vimukti-kāmā*  
*maunaṁ caranti vijane na parārtha-niṣṭhāḥ*  
*naitān vihāya kṛpaṇān vimumukṣa eko*  
*nānyaṁ tvad asya śaraṇaṁ bhramato 'nupaśye*  
*SB 7.9.44*

You remembered the divine instructions of your beloved spiritual master, Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja, and you left your idyllic existence in Vṛndāvana in order to deliver us poor fools and rascals rotting amidst the horrible noises and smells of our big cities.

The people in the West are completely devoid of God-consciousness, completely deluded by the modes of passion and ignorance. Only you could properly appreciate our abominable position. Intoxicated by sinful life, we considered ourselves very advanced. What could we learn from you? Fat and complacent, we already had so many big leaders and teachers. How could we learn anything from You?

*śrī-prahrāda uvāca*  
*matir na kṛṣṇe parataḥ svato vā*  
*mitho 'bhipadyeta gṛha-vratānām*  
*adānta-gobhir viśatāṁ tamisraṁ*  
*punaḥ punaś carvita-carvaṇānām*  
*SB 7.5.30*

You had come to the West out of compassion for us rascals, but how to save us?

*na te viduḥ svārtha-gatiṁ hi viṣṇuṁ*  
*durāśayā ye bahir-artha-māninaḥ*  
*andhā yathāndhair upanīyamānās*  
*te 'pīśa-tantryām uru-dāmni baddhāḥ*  
*SB 7.5.31*

We are so fallen and Godless, how could we ever understand your sublime message?

*naiṣāṁ matis tāvad urukramāṅghriṁ*  
*spṛśaty anarthāpagamo yad-arthaḥ*  
*mahīyasāṁ pāda-rajo-'bhiṣekaṁ*  
*niṣkiñcanānāṁ na vṛṇīta yāvat*  
*SB 7.5.32*

We think that only by your causeless mercy could such wretched souls be engaged in the loving service of the Lord—only by your transcendental tricks and by the dust of your lotus feet. You sat under a tree in Thompkins Square Park and began chanting "Hare Kṛṣṇa." And, when we thought, "Who is this fellow? What is he doing?" You began to explain the *Bhagavad-gitā As It Is*. You gave us a new life, and you taught us how to live. Some people have hearts like stone, some have hearts like gold, and some have hearts like butter. But your heart is like nectar, always melting in love of God. Therefore you were pleased by our simple efforts to dance and chant "Hare Kṛṣṇa," and to honor the *kṛṣṇa-prasāda*you prepared for us. Only by your causeless mercy could we get any idea of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and only through your transcendental love could our attachment for Śrī Kṛṣṇa develop. You gave us life and you taught us how to live. You taught us how to eat, how to sleep, how to wash, how to dress, how to worship, how to sit, how to speak, how to think, and how to serve. You gave us the "Hare Kṛṣṇa" *mantra* and the transcendental seed of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*. The sunshine of your purity and the rainfall of your mercy created auspicious conditions in which the seed could grow, and you tendered the young creeper, cutting the weeds with your sharp criticism, and supporting the sapling with your loving guidance.

What could we offer to Kṛṣṇa? Kṛṣṇa is *ātmārāma*. Furthermore, He resides in a limitless kingdom of spiritual gems, where He is always served with great reverence and affection by hundreds and thousands of goddesses of fortune. What could we ever do to please Him? Your heart is so soft that you were pleased even by the awkward attempts of His child trying to walk. And somehow or other, because you were pleased, we think that Lord Kṛṣṇa, out of His great love for you, may also have been pleased. Thus by your mercy only He granted us devotional service.

Although from one point of view we are still helpless children and can only cry for you, from another point of view you have trained us so nicely and expertly that we now feel we want to do something for you. You have given us life, but you are so kind that you want to bring everyone to their real understanding of life. Your mission is so great and we are so insignificant, but still you have engaged us in preaching—in the same spirit expressed by Śrī Prahlāda Mahārāja and in the same spirit demonstrated by your guru-mahārāja when he sent you to the West.

*taṁ śyena-vegaṁ śata-candra-vartmabhiś*  
*carantam acchidram upary-adho hariḥ*  
*kṛtvāṭṭa-hāsaṁ kharam utsvanolbaṇaṁ*  
*nimīlitākṣaṁ jagṛhe mahā-javaḥ*  
*SB 7.9.28*

We pray to remain eternally engaged in your transcendental loving service and to help give others the same life that you have given us.

Your eternal servants,

## Boston

**Part I**

In the spirit of enjoyment  
We pedestaled genitals  
Chewed thorns  
Nuzzled stool  
And tottered under an awesome load.

Blind, we saw happiness everywhere  
Bound up, we struggled  
Foolish we languished  
Eternally we died.

Illusion was our happiness, reality, life.  
We were trapped  
Shackled  
Imprisoned.

In a day without light  
In a universe without sun  
We were most important  
We were crass, filthy, self-centered,  
lowborn, liars.  
Haughtily strutting  
Through the cosmopolitan monotone  
of scum  
We pranced, insignificant specks. In those lifeless depths  
We thought we were God.

On the treadmill  
We confidently ran  
With Kali conviction  
Further into darkness  
Life after life  
Death after death  
Smiling all the while  
Lost.

**Part II**

And beyond that material cloud  
On the other side of this tiny earth globe  
Under the shelter of Śrī Śrī Rādhā Dāmodara  
In pristine purity  
You patiently sat  
In paramparā surrender  
To Bhaktisiddhānta's soul-saving order  
Translating of the Vedas  
That most delectable fruit  
Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.

Guardian of the Caitanya tree  
With saffron flowing robes  
You, triumphant guru  
Then graciously offered us the intoxicating  
fragrant flower  
Śri-hari-nāma  
Graciously then you placed before us the  
irresistable fruit  
Pure love of Godhead.  
And by your causeless mercy The crows became swans.

You saturate our eyes with heart-melting  
Śrī-vigraha  
And our tongues never know the end  
Of your most special gift  
The Lord's prasāda.

You are the most esteemed ambassador  
Our only contact  
You are the crystalline looking glass  
Our only glimpse  
Of the supreme abode.

Your touchstone mercy  
Our only foothold  
The supreme magician  
You turn bell metal to gold.

**Part III**

By the grace of our spiritual master  
Our every step  
Is hallowed and steady  
Returning back home  
Through that long-forgotten door  
Sevana.

By the grace of our spiritual master Chills of eagerness  
Wrench us from this body  
As we fall flat hundred times,  
thousand times  
Eternally.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, eternal father  
We are dogs.  
How can we praise you?  
Still, boundless is your mercy.  
Though we grovel, spit and foam  
in wrath  
Still your cleansing rain  
Purifies even the lowest born.

Master, regent, king  
Your causeless mercy,  
Your boundless grace,  
Your eternal care...  
Yet we still turn away.

Prabhupada... please!

Devotees of New Guṇḍicā Dhāma (Boston Yatra)

## Buffalo

*ohe!*  
*vaiṣṇava ṭhākura, doyāra sāgara,*  
*e dāse korunā kori'*  
*diyā pada-chāyā, śodho he āmāya,*  
*tomāra caraṇa dhori*

*chaya vega domi', chaya doṣa śodhi',*  
*chaya guna deho' dāse*  
*chaya sat-saṅga, deho' he āmāre,*  
*bosechi saṅgera āśe*

*ekakī āmāra, nāhi pāya bala,*  
*hari-nāma-saṅkīrtane*  
*tumi kṛpā kori', śraddhā-bindu diyā,*  
*deho' kṛṣṇa-nāma-dhane*

*kṛṣṇa se tomāra, kṛṣṇa dite pāro,*  
*tomāra śakati āche*  
*āmi to' kāṅgāla, 'kṛṣṇa' 'kṛṣṇa' boli',*  
*dhāi tava pāche pāche*

Oh, venerable Vaiṣṇava, dearly beloved spiritual master, you are the life and sustenance of our existence. Just as the sun activates lifeless trees at winter's end and ushers in the flower-bearing spring, you have by your presence given life to a lifeless world. Please give us the shade of your lotus feet.

Oh, ocean of auspicious qualities, please wash away our faults and passions, and as the cloud pours water on the forest fire, extinguish this blazing fire of material existence in which we have been burning since time immemorial. Please give us the shelter of your lotus feet.

Oh, lionhearted and fearless father, out of your causeless kindness you have informed us of our eternal blissful nature as servants of Kṛṣṇa. Now please bestow upon us the faith to carry on this *saṅkīrtana*movement, which is benefitingall of humanity. Please give us the protection of your lotus feet.

Oh, Śrīla Prabhupāda, protector of fallen souls, please accept our humble offerings of homage and make our lives successful. If you do not accept our tiny offerings, then our lives are ruined and we will surely be lost again in the whirlpool of material suffering. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us the mercy of your lotus feet.

Your humble servants in Buffalo, New York

## Caracas

When you were in Caracas, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you made a statement that Caracas is a *Saṅkīrtana*Temple. We have been trying to fulfill this statement by dedicating our lives to the preaching of Kṛṣṇa consciousness here in Venezuela.

I remember in your room in Māyāpur this year, many devotees were informing you of different activities of ISKCON around the world. We introduced our top distributor to you, and you remarked, "Yes, he has the mercy of Kṛṣṇa." Then you continued by stating that actually everyone has the mercy of Kṛṣṇa, but in one sense the preaching of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is higher:

*na ca tasmān manuṣyeṣu kaścin me priya-kṛttamaḥ*  
*bhavitā na ca me tasmād anyaḥ priyataro bhuvi*  
*Bg 18.69*

**"There is no servant in this world more dear to Me than him, nor will there ever be one more dear."**

Because actually you are the one who is doing the preaching, you are the most dear to Kṛṣṇa.

*bhārata-bhūmite haila manuṣya janma yāra*  
*janma sārthaka kari' kara para-upakāra*  
*Ādi 9.41*

**"One who has taken birth as a human being in the land of India should make his life successful, and work for the benefit of all other people."**

This is the instruction given by Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and you have taken up this mission with full faith in your guru-mahārāja. You have shown us that our real home is also in the transcendental land of Bharata-varṣa, in the transcendental *dhāmas*of Māyāpur and Vṛndāvana. Therefore we must also accept this instruction of Lord Caitanya as applying to ourselves. Therefore we should dedicate our lives to spreading your words to every town and village in the universe. Actually everyone is a resident of these transcendental dhāmas, and you have made it our duty to reveal this to the world.

Therefore you have so many projects; book distribution, temple construction in India, food distribution, farm projects throughout the world, and many others; and all of these projects have one aim, to fulfill the desire of your guru-mahārāja by gradually introducing everyone to Kṛṣṇa consciousness and providing occupations for all classes of men under Kṛṣṇa's *varṇāśrama-dharma*system so that everyone can go back to home, back to Godhead.

Kṛṣṇa states in the *Bhagavad-gitā* that the wise should not disturb the ignorant in their performance of *vikarmic*activities (Bg. 3.29), but you are more kind than Kṛṣṇa Himself, so we pray that you may benedict us with an atom of your infinite compassion for the conditioned souls, that we may be pleasing to you.

*tāhale ānanda hoy tabe hoy digvijay*  
*caitanyer kṛpā atiśay*  
*māyā duṣṭa jata duḥkhī jagate sabāi sukhī*  
*vaiṣṇaver icchā pūrṇa hoy*

**"As the vast mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu conquers all directions, a flood of transcendental ecstasy will certainly cover the land. When all the sinful, miserable living entities become happy, the Vaiṣṇava's desire is then fulfilled."**

So please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us become the instruments for fulfilling this desire of yours wherever you send us.

*Your servants birth after birth,*

## Carriere

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our worthless obeisances. Please accept our insufficient gratitude for bringing us out of *māyās'*blazing fire and into the cool shade of Your Divine Grace's lotus feet. Whenever we try to express our appreciation of you it is embarrasing, because our gratitude can never equal what you are doing for us.

One time you came to visit us here at your New Tālavana farm community. We were able to hear you speak and see you face-to-face and experience your presence directly (*vapu* association). However, since then we are more and more experiencing how you are present in your instructions and we are still relishing your association (*vāṇī* association).

Actually, this is the most mercy. When you go somewhere not everyone will be able to go to your room to speak with you, but you are readily available to everyone in your books. The mercy and association of a pure devotee and the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa are equally available to all in your teachings. Now, we do not want to let this priceless opportunity slip through our fingers.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, in the *Caitanya-caritāmrta* you said that "The service of the spiritual master is essential. If there is no chance to serve the spiritual master directly, a devotee should serve him by remembering his instructions. There is no difference between the spiritual master's instructions and the spiritual master himself. In his absence, therefore, his words of direction should be the pride of the disciple."

So we humbly submit ourselves at your lotus feet to pray that our minds and hearts will become eternally absorbed in your instructions. We also pray that we will be able to make ourselves suitable instruments for executing your directions perfectly. In that way we can always relish your association.

*Your fallen servants,*The devotees of New Tālavana Farm Community

## Chandigarh

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. As long as there is sunshine in this material world, please accept our respectful obeisances.

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā*  
*dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatām*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ*  
*karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*

**“We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of providence, just so that we may accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of a human being.”** ***(Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.1.22)**

We are very fortunate to come in contact with your grace. Otherwise we were simply suffering the cruel kicks of *māyā*, being forced to serve our senses. You are such a powerful Vaiṣṇava that you are delivering the whole world.

As it is stated in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, by the grace of both the spiritual master and Kṛṣṇa, one receives the seed of devotional service (*bhakti-latā-bīja*), and by the watering process of *śravaṇaṁ kīrtanam* this plant can be made to grow all the way up to Goloka Vṛndāvana.

We can see very clearly that you possess all of the qualifications of a bona fide spiritual master. Eight basic stanzas explaining the spiritual master are given by Visvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura in his famous *Gurvaṣṭaka* prayers.

First of all you are able to deliver the fallen conditioned souls from the wheel of birth and death. So many previously sinful people are now following the restrictions of no meat-eating, no gambling, no illicit sex, and no intoxication by your teachings, and along with chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, they are becoming free from birth and death.

You are always engaged in chanting and hearing the message of Lord Caitanya. By your potency people all over the world are chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. Also in accordance with the will of Caitanya Mahāprabhu, you are spreading the teachings of *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* all over the world.

You are always engaged in worshiping the eternal forms of ŚrīŚrī Rādhā Kṛṣṇa and teaching us to do the same.

You are teaching also the simple and sublime method of eating and distributing *Kṛṣṇa-prasāda*. Before, we were engaged in eating so many sinful things. But by taking *Kṛṣṇa-prasāda* we can become purified in body, mind and soul. And the process is so joyful.

You are always hearing, chanting and serving Their Lordships Śrī Rādhikā Mādhava.

By these symptoms of a bona fide spiritual master you are worshipable on an equal level with God. This is because you are teaching the message of God and are therefore very dear to the Lord. We should always remember your lotus feet and at least three times a day we should offer you our respectful obeisances unto your lotus feet.

Thank you for giving us *Bhagavad-gītā*. Thank you for giving us the work of *Saṅkīrtana*. Thank you for giving us *Kṛṣṇa-prasāda*. Thank you for giving us the association of devotees.

As stated by Uddhava to Lord Kṛṣṇa in the Eleventh Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*: "O my Lord! Transcendental poets and experts in spiritual science could not fully express their indebtedness to You, even if they were endowed with the prolonged lifetime of Brahmā, for You appear in two features—externally as the *ācārya*and internally as the Supersoul—to deliver the embodied living being by directing him how to come to You." (*Bhāg.* 11.29.6)

All glories to your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your unworthy servants at ISKCON Chandigarh, India

## Chicago

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

This material world is full of death and darkness, and we are all blind creatures groping in the endless night. With no illumination in any direction everything seemed so hopeless and futile, but then by your appearance you revealed a new hope. This *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, the tonic for this diseased age of Kali, is unveiling a new and eternal way of life, full of light and knowledge without suffering and misery. It is indeed very fortunate for us that you have translated and published these books for the general use of the Western public. Where else can such books be found? Every page is filled with the glories of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, whose pastimes and activities are wonderful to hear and recite. Full of transcendental subject matter, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* exposes the mirage existence of the material world and redirects the reader to his proper path, the path back to home, back to Godhead. With your clear purports to eliminate any tint of speculation, these books are presented so nicely that anyone will marvel at the true clarity and realization behind your every word.

Prabhupāda, these books are your mercy to the fallen conditioned souls and the foodstuff of your disciples. Our lives are maintained in so many ways by the association of these books. We think that these books are the greatest boon to humanity. Thank you for your kindness and compassion. We offer repeated obeisances unto you. We hope that you will continue on with this wonderful mission of translating the entire *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. Please bless us with just a speck of your enthusiasm for book distribution, so that we may gain your pleasure.

## Cleveland

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**"I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanya-deva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism."**

All glories unto you, O spiritual master. It has been said by Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Thākura:

*saṁsāra-dāvānala-līḍha-loka-*  
*trāṇāya kāruṇya-ghanāghanatvam*  
*prāptasya kalyāṇa-guṇārṇavasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**"The spiritual master is receiving benediction from the ocean of mercy. Just as a cloud pours water on a forest fire to extinguish it, so the spiritual master delivers the materially afflicted world by extinguishing the blazing fire of material existence. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is an ocean of auspicious qualities."**

These are the symptoms of a bona fide spiritual master, and anyone can see that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, out of your causeless mercy and kindness, are delivering so many fallen souls from this blazing fire of material existence.

In material consciousness, we have found no peace, no happiness, no satisfaction, but now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have hope, because we have tasted a higher pleasure and that pleasure is simply to try to please you and Kṛṣṇa.

Please accept this Vyāsa-Pūjā offering from the devotees here at Cleveland ISKCON, because without your blessings, our lives are spoiled.

*Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

OM TAT SAT

## Costa Rica

It is revealed in the scriptures there is no other way  
than to seek a master and at his feet stay.  
Submissively and sincerely we must submit  
so that Kṛṣṇa in our heart may someday sit.  
We should see ourselves clear of this abominable existence  
so that unto his words there will be no resistance.  
Now there are many gurus with different teachings  
for those in their hearts have different reachings.  
He must come from Kṛṣṇa in the disciplic succession  
then we'll be sure to find no repression.  
Well versed in the scriptures in perfect control of his senses  
he'll be able to help us over māyā's fences.  
A mind of good qualities and extremely kind  
there's no one like Prabhupāda we can find.  
I pray to be with you through your instructions forever  
so that this hard knot in my heart may some day sever.

Devotees of New Vaikuṇṭha Dhāma

## Costa Rica Farm

Even though I am completely unqualified and more insignificant than an ant, still I am aspiring for your causeless mercy. I am that dwarf reaching for the moon. I simply pray to Kṛṣṇa for the intelligence to remain a servant of your nice disciples eternally.

*bhavantam evānucaran nirantaraḥ*  
*praśānta-niḥśeṣa-manorathāntaraḥ*  
*kadāham aikāntika-nitya-kiṅkaraḥ*  
*praharṣayiṣyāmi sanātha-jīvitam*

**It is stated, "By serving you constantly one is freed from all material desires and is completely pacified. When shall we engage as your permanent eternal servants and always feel joyful to have such a perfect master?" (*****Stotra-ratna*** **46)**

You are like a full moon that stands out brightly amongst so many stars. This cooling and ever-beautiful light that is emanating from you is the only shelter for such a rascal as myself. My past is extremely regretful and yet my mind doesn't permit me to regret. Planting the seed of devotion in a stonehearted creature like myself is difficult, but only a pure devotee like you could do such a thing. I was bound for the deep sea of hellish distress and now I feel the fire of hope within me. Without you I wouldn't know anything. Everything I have learned worth learning I've learned from you. Therefore the *śāstras*enjoin:

*tasmād guruṁ prapadyeta jijñāsuḥ śreya uttamam*  
*śābde pare ca niṣṇātaṁ brahmaṇy upaśamāśrayam*

**"Any person who seriously desires to achieve real happiness must seek out a bona fide spiritual master and take shelter of him by initiation. The qualification of his spiritual master is that he must have realized the conclusions of the scriptures by deliberation and he must be able to convince others of these conclusions." (*****Bhāg.*** **11.3.21)**

Because of having these very rare qualifications you are the living example of a perfect devotee. You always remember Kṛṣṇa, and the exalted *sādhus* and demigods chant your glories.

There are no bounds to your mercy and there is no end to your glories. As I dwell in this fire of material consciousness I pray that you please bestow your causeless mercy upon me.

New Virajā Dhāma

Nirguṇa dāsa adhikārī / Rāma-Vigraha dāsa adhikārī / Nīlācala-devī dāsī / Mother Molly

## Dallas

The other day we saw a tiny frog swimming in the urine of a toilet that had not been flushed. He was struggling to avoid the large stools and to scale the smooth slick sides of the toilet. Wondering how he had gotten into such a predicament, it occured to us that we ourselves were in a similar situation when our spiritual master came to save us.

It is stated in the *Purāṇas*that there are many gurus who are very expert in taking away all of a disciple's money, but very rare is the guru who can take away all of his disciple's material anxieties. We are feeling very fortunate that among thousands of cheaters, the actual teacher, the *ācāryadeva*, has appeared to properly guide us, to save us from an ocean of anxiety. The *Bhāgavatam* analogizes that to take shelter of others, the cheaters, who propose themselves unceremoniously to be God or to be guru, is just like diving into shallow water. The result is that one simply breaks his head.

In order to be God, one must be *sarva-kāraṇa-kāraṇam*, the cause of all causes. Actually, the position of God has been held eternally by the Supreme Person, Bhagavān Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and it is not subject to change according to popular vote or committee appointment or any other process; neither can someone fit in as a "co-God" (i.e., "I am God, you are God, we are all God"), for the very word "God" implies the Supreme, the Ultimate, the Greatest Person. If it were possible for someone else also to come to that level, then the superlativeness of the Supreme would automatically become null and void, which it is not.

Although all conditioned souls have been vying for the position of God since time immemorial, the fact remains that it is already held in eternal tenure, and applications are not being accepted, nor will they ever be. Thus, notwithstanding the great opulences that any particular man might have, it is our recommendation that one try for a position for which he is actually suited and for which there are many openings. That position is servant of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who is *already* God.

In the same vein, one also may not be considered to be a guru, or spiritual master, if he is preaching any message other than servitude to Kṛṣṇa. He must not concoct his own idea of God consciousness or spiritual life. *Mahājano yena gataḥ sa panthāḥ* \[*Cc. Madhya* 17.186\]. He must follow in the footsteps of the great spiritual authorities who precede him.

We have seen that our own spiritual master, His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, meets the qualifications of a bona fide *guru* in that he is perfectly realized in his eternal position as servant of Kṛṣṇa and his full-time program is to implicate the rest of the world in such transcendental service. It must also be noted that his efforts have been highly successful. Now even in courts of law, the judges, lawyers, district attorneys, and grand juries are all chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. More and more it can be seen that, due to the influence of our spiritual master and his ISKCON movement, the entire world is gradually becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious. It is therefore our fervent prayer that we may be as instrumental as possible in cooperating with this movement and providing to Śrīla Prabhupāda, in his gargantuan endeavors, whatever microscopic assistance that we can.

Dallas ISKCON

## Denver

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande paramānanda-mādhavam*  
*Cc. Madhya 17.80*

I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, the deliverer of the fallen souls. His mercy turns the dumb into eloquent speakers and enables the lame to cross mountains.

His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda is the life of the world, the only light in a dark universe. Without his shelter the whole world is wandering aimlessly in ignorance, fretting on the stage of human life and then disappearing beneath the waves of animal existence. But he knows everything! Nothing is inaccessible to him. His devotion is pure and he stands at the summit of existence. Yet in patient humility and compassion he left the serenity of Vṛndāvana and alone struggled against all obstacles to reclaim us. This is a very deep thing and cannot be lightly described. We must carefully consider that without him there is no value to anything in this world. There is no measure for the weight of our debt to him. We can only work with all our energy and in all sincerity to try to help him in his glorious mission. All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

**All the residents of New Badarinārāyana**

## Detroit

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Once, people had brains in their heads instead of stool. In those days the value of a bona fide spiritual master was readily understood. No one was accepted as a respectable gentlemen unless he had been thoroughly educated by a legitimate *guru*; thus young children were placed in the care of a learned spiritual master is a matter of course. Now such good sense is rarely found. Rendered foolish by this age, even the brightest men can see no use for an *ācārya*. They are confident. They are self-reliant. They are industrious—and they are completely blind. Consequently they are falling into the proverbial ditch; which is, incidentally, quite large enough to accommodate all of them. The energy and enthusiasm of these blind men is marvelous to see, as they rush headlong toward the brink—but it does make for harder landings. Overall, the situation is very, very sad indeed.

Fortunately for such misguided men, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who is the father of everyone, is forever alert. Situated within each heart as the Supersoul, He is acutely aware of the sufferings of His sons within this material world. Out of compassion for them, to mitigate their pains, and to arrange for their transfer to the all-blissful kingdom of God, He sends His confidential representative, the bona fide spiritual master. As a nation sends its fully empowered ambassador to represent its interests in a foreign land, so the Vaikuṇṭha ambassador, the empowered *ācārya*, represents the Godhead within this material world. He embodies all the transcendental excellences found in the Lord, and is as fully capable a deliverer as the Lord Himself. One who foolishly takes the spiritual master to be an ordinary man thus makes the most dangerous mistake. *Guru Kṛṣṇa-rūpa hana, Ādi* 1.45. "The *guru* is as good as Kṛṣṇa."

No one can fathom the transcendental qualities of the spiritual master; but of all his qualities, it is compassion which is the foremost. He cannot bear to see the poor souls wandering blindly in this dangerous material world, where even sighted men must watch their step. His heart goes out to them and he considers, "If someone is ignorant and addicted to the path of *saṁsāra*, how can one who is actually learned, merciful and advanced in spiritual knowledge engage him in fruitive activity and thus further entangle him in material existence? If a blind man is walking down the wrong path, how can a gentleman allow him to continue on his way to danger? How can he approve this method? No wise or kind man can allow this." (*Bhāg.* 5.5.18) Thinking like this, the spiritual master undertakes the rescue of all the fallen souls. One after another he arrests them, pulls them away from the precipice of sense gratification, and places them safely on the broad path of devotional service.

No sooner has this kindness been done, however, than the ingrates who have been saved turn on the rescuer to protest his interference. "Why have you checked our free movements? Have you no consideration for the rights of others? We'll have you jailed, or worse!" In this way the spiritual master has taken up a thankless and dangerous task, full of insult, injury and sometimes death. There are so many examples in history of saintly persons who were abused by those whom they tried to deliver: Lord Jesus Christ, Haridāsa Thākura, Lord Nityānanda, etc. Despite these difficulties, however, the pure devotees persist in their efforts to engage everyone in the service of the Supreme Lord, which brings immediate relief from all the miseries of material existence. Such kind and self-sacrificing souls are very rare. Although they are sometimes misunderstood by fools and rascals, their work does not go unnoticed; for Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself takes up the task of glorifying His pure devotees. In the *Gītā* the Lord declares, "For one who explains the supreme secret to the devotees, devotional service is guaranteed, and at the end he will come back to Me. There is no servant in this world more dear to Me than he, nor will there ever be one more dear." \[Bg. 18.69\]

With such an endorsement by the Supreme Personality of Godhead, it is sure and certain that the fame of the pure devotee spiritual master will spread unchecked all over the world; the order of Lord Kṛṣṇa cannot be ignored. At His command, all heads must bow down at the feet of the recognized spiritual master. Practically we are now seeing it to be so; for our own beloved spiritual master. His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, is gradually becoming the most famous and worshiped personality in the world. Despite the envious efforts of his cripple-minded critics, Śrīla Prabhupāda's influence is spreading more and more. His books are now found everywhere, and are being studied by the most learned scholars. Millions of people are visiting his palatial temples all over the world, and bowing down before his *vyāsāsana*. And best of all, people everywhere are taking up the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa, simply by his humble request, "Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and be happy."

As disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda we have arranged for his glorification today in the form of this Vyāsa-pūjā book, as well as by celebrations in all of our ISKCON temples. The real mastermind of this universal tribute, however, is Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself. It is His incontrovertible will and pleasure that the name and fame of His pure devotee be glorified everywhere. In the face of such perfect expertise and determination, our immature contribution can hardly be considered significant. Still, we are hopeful that when our feeble words are added to those of thousands and millions of more qualified devotees, the resultant sound vibration will draw the attention and reverence of every intelligent man. May they all come and bow down before Śrīla Prabhupāda. He is the foremost representative of Lord Caitanya and the spiritual master of everyone. Today is his eighty-second birthday; we therefore prostrate ourselves in the dust at his lotus feet and offer him our most humble obeisances.

Detroit, Michigan Devasādhana Mandira

## Dixon

From the devotees from the New Godruma Farm Community to our beloved Gurudeva on this most blessed day: Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are receiving benediction from the ocean of mercy. Just as the cloud pours water on the forest fire to extinguish it, so you are extinguishing the blazing fire of material existence, of repeated birth and death. Our lives are caught in a cesspool of sinful activities, so what good can come of it? We approach you, Gurudeva, with straw in teeth, because Kṛṣṇa declares that you have seen the truth. Give us sight, let us see!!

Now we pray, we beg, please let us stay with you in your service until we understand without deviation this king of all knowledge.

Gurudeva, just give us, your servants, one drop of your mercy. We are lower than the blades of grass; give us help; give us strength. Let us be as you are without desires or aspirations.

If you are not merciful unto us, we can only weep and we will not be able to maintain our lives.

## Eastern Europe TSKP

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**"I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him."**

On this most holy day, we, with our tiny and limited ability, attempt to glorify you, whose glories know no limits. You have given us wretched souls so much help and kindness that we may go on serving you eternally, ever indebted to you. The grace which you bestow so compassionately upon everyone is called divine, because it is coming from Kṛṣṇa. You are the manifestation of Kṛṣṇa's mercy in the material world, therefore you are known as His Divine Grace.

Who can understand the wonderful characteristics of the pure devotee? When we, your disciples, look upon you, we are simply amazed, our hearts filled with respect and affection for you. Please let us never consider you to be ordinary! Please let us always remember that you do not live in any planet of the material sky, nor do you feel any contact with material elements. Your so-called material body does not exist, being surcharged with the spiritual current of the Lord's identical interest, and thus you are permanently freed from all contaminations of the sum total of the *mahat-tattva.* You are always in the spiritual sky since you constantly share the transcendental loving association of the Lord.

You have kindly accepted us as your disciples, therefore our only business is now to please you. To please the spiritual master means to receive his blessings. Without the blessings of the pure devotee no one can make spiritual advancement. Once you said that real gratitude toward you is if we simply understand the importance of this Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In this dark age of Kali, Kṛṣṇa consciousness is greatly needed, because millions of people are suffering greatly due to having lost their proper vision. They have forgotten that the living entities are eternally subordinate servants of God. Living under these conditions, how would it be possible for them to become happy? Thus everybody is thinking in the bodily conception of life and remaining like animals. Just like in eastern Europe, where the attempt is systematically made to kill God, the leaders are so afraid of the name "God" that they are very enthusiastic to make all kinds of propaganda against religion. Scientists and professors are writing volumes of books about scientific atheism. Spending millions of dollars, they have built huge arrangements for defense, to make sure that no other idea will obstruct their way. But can there be any hindrance before your *saṅkīrtana*movement? Can anyone stop this mission of Lord Caitanya? Despite all security arrangements and propaganda, can anyone remain hidden from your books, which are the illumination of perfection and purity? The words of the pure devotee penetrate all iron curtains of false propaganda. You are the intimate associate of Lord Caitanya, therefore you manifest His *saṅkīrtana-śakti*in person. Therefore no society can escape from being flooded with your books. This flood of *saṅkīrtana*sweeps away all useless security by which the demons try to avoid surrender to Kṛṣṇa. The devotees are drowned in this ocean of ecstasy, and the nondevotees are floating. In this way everybody, even the lowborn atheists, get the chance of being delivered. And all this is due to the divine attributes of the pure devotee, as it is confirmed in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*:

*te vai vidanty atitaranti ca deva-māyāṁ*  
*strī-śūdra-hūṇa-śabarā api pāpa-jīvāḥ*  
*yady adbhuta-krama-parāyaṇa-śīla-śikṣās*  
*tiryag-janā api kim u śruta-dhāraṇā ye*

**"Surrendered souls, even from the groups leading sinful lives, such as the woman, the laborer class, the hūnas, the Siberians, etc., or even the birds and beasts, can also know about the science of Godhead and become liberated from the clutches of the illusory energy by surrendering unto the pure devotee of the Lord and by following in his footsteps in devotional service." (*****Bhāg.*** **2.7.46)**

By helping you to spread this *saṅkīrtana*movement, we are doing the greatest welfare work. This is the only way to pay back a minute part of our debt to you. However, to perform this service there is need of good qualities. Unfortunately we haven't got any quality about which we could be proud. For anything really valuable we have to ask Your Divine Grace. Thus, we are a lot of beggars only.

O Gurudeva, please shower your rain of mercy upon us! Please give us strength and enthusiasm! Please let us take an eternal part in your *saṅkīrtana*movement! Please give us the chance to be with you and serve you wherever you are!

*Your spiritual sons,*

## Ecuador

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Your words  
Once they’ve struck the heart  
Of the innocent victim  
are like the venom  
that subtly cuts material life  
short !  
It is as if  
in your presence  
the incessant flow  
Of material desires  
Had no choice  
than to stop  
Even momentarily

My eyes weep  
'cause they can't see you  
and when my eyes  
behold you  
my heart quivers and throbs  
and desperately cries  
'cause my hands  
long to touch your lotus feet  
but due to my insignificant condition  
I cannot reach you.  
It is as if  
In your presence  
The incessant flow  
of material desires  
had no other choice  
Than to stop  
even if for a moment.  
Gracias,  
tus eternos sirvientes

## Fiji Islands

**Śrīla Prabhupāda "I.O.U."**

The material debts I had accumulated were too many to count.  
Lawyer, doctor and dentist, such an incredible amount!

But as much as I'd make myself debtor to all,  
by good ways and right means the sum soon did fall.

So many bills, so many ills, sometimes in the red, sometimes in the black  
But there's one debt now that I could never pay back.

The debt to my spiritual master, can anything there greater be?  
Nor could lifetimes of service remove that responsibility.

He opened my eyes which were closed tight for so long.  
An operation that required śāstra, sat-saṅga and song.

Such methods were revolutionary in light of present-day "knowledge,"  
which now only appears to be so much useless garbage.

A change of heart, a clean, fresh mind,  
is there any surgeon this day, my soul, can he find?

I know they would try hard but by material means,  
which is like trying to climb stars with "two-by-four" beams.

There's no man alive today who knows such an art,  
of cleansing the mind and melting the heart.

To you dear Prabhupāda I've fallen in debt  
with no way to reimburse the price that you've set.

You ask only simple service to Kṛṣṇa with love,  
but try as I may can a crow become a dove?

Here's all that I have, just one humble prayer,  
to convey a few words and show that I care.

Please take what I offer, though so little to give.  
Somewhere in your deep heart please grant me reprieve.

The devotees of the Fiji Islands

## Gainesville

Moon is farther than the sun,  
With this knowledge you have come.  
Souls whose lives were without fun,  
Now, however, you have won.

Words of wisdom on your lips,  
Vedic scriptures are your stick.  
Absolute Truth within your grip,  
Paramparā is your whip.

Karmīs, jñānīs are your game,  
Kṛṣṇa-bhakti is your fame.  
Smashing concepts of "all the same,"  
One way only-the holy name!

Life comes from life, you have proved.  
Scientific nonsense thus disproved.  
Devotion to Kṛṣṇa you've approved,  
Māyā's illusion now removed.

Chemicals not we are made,  
Impersonal calamity you have razed.  
God is a person we have craved,  
Road to Godhead you have paved.

Like a lion without fear,  
Bringing the message both far and near.  
Yasya deve parā bhaktiḥ,  
All that's left is just to hear.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our worthless obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. Although it is not the position of the servant to ask from the master, we are simply praying that you benedict us with the potency to preach this knowledge all over the world. Last year we saw the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement become famous in every household throughout the planet, and even. though we are now engaged in a fierce struggle with the demons who are trying to stop this movement, we are confident that this is Kṛṣṇa's plan for a glorious victory. Therefore Śrīla Prabhupāda, don't forget your fallen disciples here in Gainesville. We want to go too. Everyone loves a winner, and you are conquering the entire universe in the tradition of Prahlāda Mahārāja, Ambarīṣa Mahārāja, Arjuna and the Pāṇḍavas, Lord Caitanya and the Six Gosvāmīs, Bhaktivinoda Thākura and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta. Please take us with you. Don't leave us here to rot in this ocean of birth and death. Force us to become qualified to have your association. Please let us be there when the whole world surrenders at your lotus feet, and Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa are installed in the White House!

*Your fallen servants,*

## Guayaquil

Auspicious stars glittered in the firmament,  
In anticipation of your divine descent.  
And on the hallowed day on which you came,  
The devotees danced and sang in ecstasy the holy name.

Your devotees are like so many grains of sand,  
Benedicted by your books, and the touch of your hand,  
Your words are soft but like thunder, and your character of gold,  
The friend of the fallen, and the Vaiṣṇava bold.

You alone came to māyā's citadel,  
And began to ring the real liberty bell,  
The sleeping souls began to awaken,  
And, for the first time, were spiritually shaking!

Chant! Chant! Chant! You commanded.  
And everyone danced, completely enchanted.  
Now "Hare Kṛṣṇa" has become a household word,  
And in every city His ame is heard.

No one can be our spiritual master  
But you who have given the answer  
To "who am I?" and life's purpose  
Of being Lord Kṛṣṇa's eternal servants.

You are honored all over the three worlds,  
As the most confidential servitor of the Lord,  
And so amidst a chorus of celestial praise,  
We submit this small offering to Your Divine Grace.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servants at Guayaquil, Ecuador

## Geneva

Gurudeva,

Harassed by uncontrolled senses,  
Haunted by mental speculation,  
my heart remains a barren earth,  
unfit to receive your seed of Kṛṣṇa-prema.  
You bravely declare any land fertile;  
Undauntedly you plow away the rocks  
and water the parched cracks  
of my insignificant life.  
Eternal master,

You have brought Śrī Caitanya's blessing  
to such remote and useless spots as my heart,  
Still reaping the fruit of eternal service  
to Govinda's lotus feet.  
You are empowered to bring all bewildered souls  
back to home, back to Godhead,  
and though bereft of all merit  
I humbly implore Your Divine Grace  
to permit this worthless creature  
to assist you.

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatām*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*  
*SB 1.1.22*

For countless aeons we have languished in this world of chimera, enthralled by māyā's embrace. We have all but forgotten the lotus feet of Govinda, the primeval Lord. Had Your Divine Grace not intervened in the bleak and miserable wasteland of our life, the seed of devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa would never have been sown.

*brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*  
*Cc Madhya 19.151*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead because only through your confidential service to Lord Kṛṣṇa has our journey through myriad sinful activities and their reactions been checked and *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* enshrined. You have uplifted such foolish scoundrels as ourselves, and therefore, you too are *patita-pāvana*. We remember having accompanied Your Divine Grace on a morning walk during which your silence was punctuated by the rhythmic tapping of your cane on the ground. While we were hoping to capture your merciful glance, you suddenly turned on us and stated a Sanskrit *śloka*. the only word of which we could understand was *mleccha*. You then lapsed again into a long silence before revealing the meaning of your quotation. "You are all *mlecchas*, "you said tersely, "but by the grace of a pure devotee you have become Vaiṣṇava." And then you did give us a merciful glance.

*Vaiṣṇava thākura tomāra kukura boliyā jana*. The pure devotee is thākura, as good as God Himself because he can deliver God to all manners of men. Therefore, we simply pray to become your *kukura*, or dog. Our qualities being nonexistent, we could never have reached you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but Your Divine Grace being boundless, has nonetheless reached down to us. You have filled our body with vigor, our mind and intelligence with *rāja-vidyā*, and our heart with *prema*. We shall never be able to repay the awesome debt that we have incurred to you. Cāṇakya Paṇḍita declares that no amount of riches can ever repay one word of the spiritual master's instructions. Nevertheless, we implore you to give us the steadfastness to do your bidding so that we may begin to try. We pledge our life to your exalted mission of bringing Lord Caitanya's *nāma-saṅkīrtana* to every town and village.

*pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi grāma*  
*sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma*  
*\[CB Antya-khaṇḍa 4.126\]*

You have often affirmed that you have given us everything—the holy name, the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, Deity worship, Kṛṣṇa *prasāda*—and that it is now simply up to us to preach extensively and tell everyone enthusiastically about Kṛṣṇa. Kindly grant us the strength to follow in your footsteps, whatever the condition of life in which Kṛṣṇa may deem fit to place us.

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance in this world, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we address you with all the humility that we can muster and wish to reaffirm the vows which we made to you in front of the fire, the *mūrtis*, and the assembled devotees at the time of our initiation. Let us constantly remain your menial servants, for we have no refuge outside your lotus feet.

the devotees of ISKCON-Switzerland

## Germany

A11 the living entities in the material world are day and night very busy under the spell of the illusory energy. Seeking for happiness and peace, the living entity experiences different degrees of so-called happiness and distress. Thus wandering from one body to another in 8,400,000 species of life-sometimes accepting the body of a plant or that of an animal or sometimes of a human being or demigod—the living entity completely forgets his eternal relationship with the Lord. Foolishly he is satisfied with his material position, never experiencing the real happiness he is seeking since time immemorial. Trying to establish himself under nice living conditions, all his attempts for happiness are burnt by the all-devouring time.

*yaṁ yam artham upādatte duḥkhena sukha-hetave*  
*taṁ taṁ dhunoti bhagavān pumāñ chocati yat-kṛte*

**"Whatever is produced by the materialist with great pain and labor for so-called happiness, the Supreme Personality, as the time factor, destroys, and for this reason the conditioned soul laments."** ***(Bhāg*****. 3.30.2)**

The burning conditioned soul in the material world may get relief from its distress when getting in contact with the authorized spiritual master who is compared to the cloud that showers rain anywhere without consideration. This "rain" is the causeless mercy of the Lord and it is showered by the causeless mercy of the guru.

*saṁsāra-dāvānala-līḍha-loka-trāṇāya kāruṇya-ghanāghanatvam*  
*prāptasya kalyāṇa-guṇārṇavasya vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**"The spiritual master is receiving benediction from the ocean of mercy. Just as a cloud pours water on a forest fire to extinguish it, so the spiritual master delivers the materially afflicted world by extinguishing the blazing fire of material existence. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is an ocean of auspicious qualities."**

The *ācārya* can instruct one about the value of human life. There is no need for the disciple to be rich, beautiful or learned. Everyone can become enlightened merely by associating with the bona fide *ācārya* even if devoid of all good qualities. Similarly Nārada Muni, when born as a *śūdra*, became enlightened by the association of great devotees. *Ācāryavān puruṣo veda*. Simply by associating with devotees of the Lord one will also become a devotee, and one will use this form of life the right way. This human form of life is not meant to be wasted for sense gratification. For that purpose there are so many other forms of life. If at the time of death you can remember the Lord then your life was successful. Otherwise not. All that counts is *ante nārāyaṇa-smṛti*. Nothing else. This is the teachings of the *ācāryas*. It is our choice to take it or not. When we are sick we consult a physician who can give some good advice. He tells us "do this, don't do that, eat this, don't eat that, etc." If we seriously follow the orders of the physician then we can become cured in due course. But if we don't follow his orders then there is no chance of becoming cured.

Our disease is that we are accepting this material existence as real, but by the instructions of the *guru* we can become cured and taste real existence manifested in our eternal loving relationship with the Lord. But for that experience full surrender unto the spiritual master's instructions is required. *Yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādaḥ*. By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa.

Dear Prabhupāda, unfortunate as we are, we don't care for your instructions, but instead we are always looking for sense gratification in the material world. When will we become like Śrīla Rūpa and Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī? Although being very advanced they considered themselves to be the most fallen, and taking a straw in their mouth they fully surrendered at the lotus feet of their spiritual master, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. That is the behavior of a perfect disciple. Please engage us in your service although we will never attain such qualities as they did. Maybe, gradually we will get purified so that you can bless us with *bhaktisakti*. Then we could do some minor service for you, namely carry on your message. Gurudeva, we are fools. We are diseased but we reject the help of a physician. How can there be any hope for us? You give us gold but we prefer to take dirt. It is just your mercy that you do not throw us away, although it is a waste of time to care for us.

Your eternal servants at ISKCON Germany

## Guadalajara

The spiritual master is the bona fide representative and personal friend of the Supreme Lord, and by his mercy we can also become dear to the Lord. And because the spiritual master is the representative of the Supreme Lord, he must be honored as such. Therefore, each and every day, each and every hour, each and every moment we must remember the lotus feet of our spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda. That is the meaning of advancement in spiritual life.

Factually, before Śrīla Prabhupāda came to the west, none of us knew about Kṛṣṇa, none of us knew that the Supreme Lord is a person and has pastimes, that He has friends and a residence, and that each one of us can go back to home and play with Him. That information has only been given by our spiritual master. He has given us spiritual life, and without Śrīla Prabhupāda no one can find spiritual life. Therefore, there is no way to repay what he has given.

It is stated in the Vedic literature that when one approaches the spiritual master to acquire knowledge from him, one has to give something in return. That is called *dakṣinā*

But *dakṣinā* does not mean just a quantity of money. The only *dakṣinā* which is acceptable is one's life.

Śrīla Prabhupāda has given his life to spread this movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and has given up all the comforts of Vṛndāvana to come teach us about Kṛṣṇa. The only thing he asks is that we finish our sense gratification and chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. Therefore, let us offer our lives unto the lotus feet of our divine master. His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.
